# The analysis of the 54 goals against the goal standard of three kinds

The record of the analysis that `spec@goals-at-the-root@standard-and-rewrite` owes before any goal
is edited: for each goal its kind, the fate proposed, a row for each of its sentences with a
destination, a draft where it is kept, and the head proposed where a sentence earns one. Nothing
here is ruled. The owner rules the themes, the fate of each goal and every sentence sent nowhere,
and the rulings are written into this file before the goals are edited. It leaves in the commit
that writes the goals the owner ruled last, or a later commit of the same branch.

An item written as a hash sign and an id, such as #arg-31, is an item of
`milestone@plans@goals-at-the-root`, where the owner's words are kept. One agent analysed each theme and saw no other theme's work. A second agent per theme then tried
to refute it against the tree, and a third corrected the theme on the findings that held: a row
sent to a head that says less, a draft that dropped a bound of its goal, a sentence with no
destination. The questions are numbered Q1 to Q96 across the file; a question the correction added
carries a letter after the label of the nearest question of its goal, such as Q3a.

## The owner's rulings

Where a ruling below differs from a section of a theme, the ruling holds, and the section is read
with it.

### The themes, and the fate of each goal

The session put two items to the owner in one message: the five themes, which are also the files
of the root's goals home, and the fate of each of the 54 goals as the theme summaries below give
it, to be ruled by exception. The owner:

> I agree on Q-A, and on most of Q-B. the only point to reopen is about only-needed-instructions.
> The point of that goal is that the retrospective and the agent text audit can return findings
> about lots of edge cases, or lapses. Covering every edge cases is impossible, and judgement of
> agents is supposed to handle cases that are not covered by the workflow. without it, agent
> working autonomously tended to add isntructions to cover every edge case so that no ambiguity
> would be left in any instruction, but in practice this cannot be achieved because there is no
> limit in the situations an agent using the installed workflow may face. Maybe it is more a
> design head than a goal. Or a bit of both. The point is that the installed workflow should not
> grow without bound.
> Actually, maybe agents-get-a-complete-workflow might be better if split differently. as you say,
> the fusion makes it cover more things. It might be a deeper problem in the root goal itself. Any
> other split comes to you ?

"Q-A" is the themes and "Q-B" the fates; both labels were numbered in that message alone. So the
themes are ruled as proposed, and every fate is ruled as proposed but those the next two rulings
change. The wording of each kept goal is not ruled: it goes through the goal reviewer and the
rounds of the whole-set review.

### The workflow goal is split in three

The session proposed, in place of the merge of `goal@agent-skills@only-needed-instructions` into
`goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`:

1. `goal@agent-skills@only-needed-instructions` stands alone, as a scope: the installed workflow
   states what an agent needs to carry out its activities, and leaves every other case to the
   agent's judgement. It is a goal of its own because it bounds the installed text whichever goal
   an added instruction would serve, the complete workflow, no drift, better code or the owner's
   goals, so it means something without any one purpose, which is the test of rule 5. It is a goal
   and not a head because the growth it excludes comes by additions that each win on a local
   argument, and a head is a presumption that a better local argument reverses.
2. `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow` keeps what agents get, a workflow
   for each generic activity the owner has taken in, which an agent carries out with the workflow
   and what its project adds, there to raise the quality and the efficiency of the agents' work.
   It takes in the list of activities of `goal@agent-skills@one-skill-per-activity` and the one
   clause of `goal@agent-skills@installed-text-works-in-any-environment`, and no property of the
   text.
3. The properties of the text are design heads: that no two installed instructions contradict and
   each is plain enough to follow, so `goal@agent-skills@no-contradiction-in-workflow` is a head, as
   the first sorting had it; the clause on structure the work does not need, which falls from a
   goal's weight to a presumption; and how "needs" is tested.

The session told the owner three costs: one goal more; the clause on structure becoming a
presumption; and that no goal protects an instruction by naming its act once measures leave, the
need test alone doing so. The owner:

> 1. I think this is right.
> 2. agreed

"1" is the split, and "2" that the two drafts the session showed give the shape only, their
wording going through the goal reviewer.

### The other merges are read again for a better split

The owner then asked, and the session agreed, that each other merge the analysis proposes be read
with the same question: whether the merged statement bounds or serves more than the purpose it
joins, follows from it by an argument, or is a qualifier of it. The owner:

> Maybe we should apply the same question on splitting to the other proposals of the review: they
> proposed to merge, but the merged goals, having grown in scope, might admit a better splitting
> under a new shape, whether as multiple goals or as goals + design.

The reading was made by one agent and contested by a second. Its result, as put to the owner:

| merge the analysis proposed | after the reading |
| --- | --- |
| `goal@knowledge-architect@owner-attention-on-what-is-theirs` into `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` | does not stand, and is no goal of its own as drafted: its sentences are held by `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`, `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts` and `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`; no goal holds a bound on the owner's reading time |
| `goal@knowledge-architect@records-live-beside-the-code` into `goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments` | a new design head of the root's design home, argued from three goals |
| `goal@agent-skills@installed-text-works-in-every-target-project` into `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` | design: `design@agent-skills@shipped-text-cites-no-entry` holds part, the rest a new head or the Component's scoped instructions |
| `goal@create@a-project-adopts-by-one-command` into `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy` | split: "by one command" is `design@create@init-command`; "loses nothing the project wrote" was put to the owner as a scope of its own |
| `goal@knowledge-architect@structure-and-workflow-work-together` into `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` | nothing enters the receiving goal; one home per kind of record is a head argued from it |
| `goal@check@documents-stay-current` and `goal@check@document-rules-are-checked` into `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` | stands, but the sentence that a project declares to the checker what it records, which `design@check@nothing-of-a-project-is-compiled-in` argues |
| `goal@model@recorded-items-can-be-referenced` into `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` | stands as a merge; whether the owner's example of a documentation model and checker is a goal of its own was put to the owner |
| `goal@query@agents-query-the-project` into `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s` | stands |
| `goal@knowledge-architect@setup-brings-quality-tools` into `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped` | design: four existing heads hold it |

The session put three questions: whether the owner holds a bound on the owner's reading time,
with a default of no; whether "what knowledge-architect writes into a project loses nothing the
project wrote" is a goal, with a default of yes; and whether the owner's example of a
documentation model and checker is a goal of its own, with a default of yes. The owner:

> q1: I have no bound to state on the quantity of what reaches me, and I prefer not too.  As long
> as there is value I can bring whenever an item is brought to me. For example, a question about a
> missing fact, a cost weighting, or a goal reading. If there is nothing else I can give other
> than approval, there is little value in bring the question to me. BTW, how do the current rules
> on autonomous decisions handle one that turns on a goal reading ? I think this case was not
> really existent up until now, because goals were worded to leave no ambiguosity.  We should
> handle it so these cases get reported to me. And maybe this needs a test to decide what is meant
> by "turns on a goal reading"
> Under our new standard, there should now be less goals and more design, so overall less for me
> to rule on. Maybe initially, when design gets filled to fulfill goals, and new goal readings and
> technical solutions to meet them still need to be decided. but once that settles, I believe
> agents will be able to work relatively autonomously.
> Q2: this is probably not worth a goal IMO. This is more of a small, orthogonal design decision,
> because that happening would arguably be bad ux.
> Q3: yes, I think it is. I think the way I framed it at this time could lead to a better list of
> goals overall.
> The starting point is that we define a documentation structure, a model representation in Rust
> and a scanner. This is the starting point of the other goals: some add content to the model
> (citations, design entries, issues...) while others add features that work upon it: the checker,
> the render...
> Beside that, we also ship an agent workflow, which has its own goals. And both of these aspects
> of the project are supposed to work together, the agents record knowledge in the modeled
> documentation, while tools are built upon the model (or outside of it)  to make agents more
> efficient at searching, updating, and verifying knowledge.
> The existing goals were mostly describing that, maybe not in exactly the same order and splits.

So: no goal bounds the owner's reading time; that a tool's writes lose nothing the project wrote
is a design decision and no goal; and the documentation model is a goal of its own, from which
the owner proposes to order the whole list.


### The list of goals, in six files, ordered from the documentation model

On the owner's framing above, the session proposed that the owner's paragraph becomes the head of
the root's goals home, in place of its paragraph on two main purposes, and that the home holds 21
goals in six files. It asked whether "in Rust" is part of the first goal's purpose. The list, by
title and origin, its wording not ruled:

| file | goal | from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the documentation model | a project's documentation has a defined structure, and a model of it that tools read | new, the owner's example; it takes the declaring of kinds of entries, the naming and citing of a recorded item, and the reading that judges nothing of `goal@model@a-tool-reads-the-documents-with-no-check` |
| the documentation model | the documentation records the design, with the intent and the arguments behind it | `goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments` |
| the documentation model | a recorded item reaches the work it bears on | `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` |
| what is built on the model | the documentation stays consistent with the code and with itself | `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` |
| what is built on the model | a project adds checks of its own | `goal@check@projects-add-their-own-checks` |
| what is built on the model | the documents render as one linked site | `goal@knowledge-architect@documents-render-as-a-linked-site` |
| what is built on the model | a task a tool does at a lower cost, or better, is the tool's | `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`, widened |
| the workflow | agents get a workflow for each generic activity taken in | `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`, thinner |
| the workflow | the workflow states what an agent needs and leaves the rest to judgement | `goal@agent-skills@only-needed-instructions`, a scope |
| the workflow | the workflow leads agents to write better code | `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code` |
| the workflow | the workflow leads a project to be split into modular units | `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-components`, a wider wording |
| the workflow | the workflow improves through real use | `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`, thinner |
| the workflow | the main session orchestrates the work | `goal@agent-skills@session-orchestrates-the-work` |
| the two together | agents work autonomously without drifting from the recorded design | `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`, with its ranking |
| the two together | the generic part of a tool or a procedure is shipped once taken in | `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped` |
| the owner and the agents | three goals | `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`, `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-s-word-is-verified`, `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts` |
| whom the project serves, and adoption | three goals | `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`, `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`, `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy` |

Two fates ruled earlier change with it: the statement of
`goal@model@a-tool-reads-the-documents-with-no-check` is part of the first goal, and the sentence of
`goal@knowledge-architect@structure-and-workflow-work-together` is the sentence of the home's head
that joins the two parts. The six files replace the five themes. The owner:

> 2. agreed on those 6 files and 21 goals in the list.
> 3. design under the scope goal. Since we are ourselves a target project, in rust is required.

So the first goal names no language: that the model is a Rust library follows from the scope goal.

### A decision that turns on a reading of a goal is reported with the reading

The owner asked how a decision that turns on a reading of a goal is handled, and that such cases
be reported. The session proposed a test and an instruction for `primer@decided-section`. The
test: a decision turns on a reading of a goal when the goal is its ground against a rival, and the
rival would survive under another reading that the goal's words admit. The instruction: such a
decision is reported under "Decided" with the goal, the reading taken and the rival it defeats; it
is still the session's, on its argument; and where no argument ranks two readings that move in
different directions, it goes to the owner. The owner:

> 1 agreed on the reporting of a goal reading

It is built on this branch through the design skill's in-change path, and is no part of the
goals' rewrite.

## Theme: The record and its documents

Questions Q1 to Q17.

Branch `goals/standard-and-rewrite`, tree at the commit of the design audit of this slice. 15 goals: 7 of the root's goals home,
the 5 of `check`, the 3 of `model`. No file of the repository was edited.

How to read the tables:

- A row is one sentence, or one list item, of the goal's body. Long sentences are quoted by their
  first and last words.
- "verified" after a head means I read the head's text and it says what the row says. "title
  only" means I read its title and the line that cites the goal, not its whole body.
- An Admits case that the reworded statement still admits, and that no head holds, is sent
  `nowhere: repeats the statement`: an Admits case binds nothing, so nothing is lost. I list this
  choice under "Decided".
- A line "It refines ..." is sent `nowhere: held by` the goal-setting skill, whose rule 8 removes
  the line from every goal. Where the line carries a reason, the reason has a row of its own.
- The questions are labelled `Q1` to `Q17`, numbered within this file. The dispatcher renumbers
  them in the record they join.

Words of the owner that bear on the whole theme, quoted once here and cited below as "the owner's
two examples". Milestone document, round 2, `arg-31`:

> If I add the goal that we ship a documentation model and checker that allow declaring various
> types of entries and citing them, we have most of the current shape. Then I add that these model
> and checker are supposed to serve a project keeping design documentation up to date with code, in
> a project developed by LLM agents, and we have the current shape that appears, with the current
> enforced categories of entries, such as goals, design, rejected...

These are the only words of the owner in the milestone document that state a purpose of this theme.
Example A is "a documentation model and checker that allow declaring various types of entries and
citing them". Example B is "a project keeping design documentation up to date with code, in a
project developed by LLM agents".

### `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`: Agents work productively and autonomously without drifting from the design recorded in their project

**Kind**: purpose, with a ranking written inside it. Removed, the reviews that read work against the
record, the record search and the register of designs not kept would have no reason left to exist.
The ranking paragraph is a second intent under rule 5: it means something without the purpose's
text.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded), as the purpose. The ranking leaves the body and becomes a
ranking goal of its own, or a sentence of the home's head, which already says "the first ranks
above the second": Q1. The milestone document's first mapping table has no row for this goal.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

- Round 2, `arg-30`: "And there is a last wall of reviews before merge, including transcript
  reviewers, which make sure that significant decision reversals are not happening behind my back
  anyway."
- Round 2, `arg-28`: "It improves agent autonomy, and it should not in practice result in problems,
  because in practice this decision can only be reversed on a better argument."
- Round 2, `arg-31`, example B above.
- None on the ranking, and none on the list of what it "never ranks over". The rewrite must ask.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 2 | "What they build stays within the design recorded in that project, as it stands until a change reverses part of it through the workflow's procedure:" with its three items: "its goals", "the decisions its record holds, with their arguments", "the reasons recorded beside its code and its agent-facing text" | the outcome; the three items say what "the design recorded" is | stays in the goal; "through the workflow's procedure" is reworded as an outcome, a reversal made on purpose and argued |
| 3 | "What they build brings back none of the designs its record did not keep, unless a change reopens one through the workflow's procedure." | the outcome, second half | stays in the goal |
| 4 | "Where two goals compete, it ranks first among knowledge-architect's goals, every Component's included." | a ranking | stays in the goal, as the ranking goal of Q1; "every Component's included" goes nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@skills/knowledge-architect-goal-setting/SKILL.md`, "Where the goals sit", under which no Component states a goal |
| 5 | "A goal that refines it takes no rank from it." | a rule on refining goals | nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@skills/knowledge-architect-goal-setting/SKILL.md`, rule 8: no goal refines another, so the sentence has no case left |
| 6 | "It never ranks over:" "a goal's exclusion that names the design it excludes;" | a scope prevails over this purpose | nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@skills/knowledge-architect-goal-setting/SKILL.md`, rule 1: a scope "names the design it excludes, one that a purpose would otherwise argue for", so a scope bounds every purpose by its kind. Shown to the owner: Q2 |
| 7 | "the owner's authority, exchange and attention that [three goals] give, as the goals that refine them state it;" | this purpose never ranks over the owner's part | stays in the goal, as the ranking goal of Q1, without "as the goals that refine them state it". The three goals belong to another theme: Q2 |
| 8 | "the check of the owner's word that `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-s-word-is-verified` asks;" | the same, for the check of the owner's word | stays in the goal, as the ranking goal of Q1. The goal named belongs to another theme: Q2 |

Second half: nowhere: held by `primer@goals-bind`: "A decision binds as a presumption", and where a decision conflicts with a goal "the goal prevails", so an ask that a design decision states ranks as that decision with no sentence of a goal. The analysis of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`, in another theme, has no row for this sentence, so this row is its only one. Shown to the owner: Q2a |
| 10 | "It is met while the workflow is built, and learns from its retrospectives, so that, in a target project, a departure from that recorded design, or a design not kept brought back without that procedure, is caught before it merges." | the measure | in three parts. For a plan document: existing head: `design@agent-skills@plan-read-against-the-record` (verified: it decides one reviewer of plan documents, and quotes this measure as the goal's, with the stale words "a rejected design brought back" that the slice's audit subjects name; the one pass rewrites the quotation). For a branch whose record search gave a verdict that changes the work: existing head: `design@agent-skills@verdicts-reach-the-conformance-review` (verified: the axis is dispatched "at the review before the merge of a branch whose message carries one with a verdict other than `constrains` and `unrelated`"). For a departure in any other work: design issue, as row 13: no head argues a review that catches it at every merge. "learns from its retrospectives" is stated by `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`, of another theme: "The retrospective collects the cases where the installed text failed what it is for" |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **Agents carry a project's work autonomously, without drifting from the design recorded in it** (draft slug: agents-work-without-drift)
>
> knowledge-architect lets AI agents carry a target project's work with less supervision. What they
> build stays within the design the project has recorded: its goals, its decisions with their
> arguments, and the reasons recorded beside its code and its agent-facing text. A recorded decision
> changes only by a change that argues its reversal, and a design the project did not keep comes
> back only by a change that reopens it on purpose.

**Draft of the ranking**, if the owner keeps it as a goal of its own (draft, not ruled; Q1):

> **Where two purposes compete, autonomy without drift comes first, after the owner's part** (draft slug: drift-ranks-first)
>
> Where two purposes of knowledge-architect compete, letting agents work without drifting from the
> recorded design comes first. It never comes before what the goals give the owner: the owner's
> decisions, the exchange with the owner, the owner's attention, and the check of the owner's word.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q1**. The ranking is a second intent. Is it (a) a ranking goal of its own, as drafted, or (b)
  the sentence of the home's head, "It has two main purposes, and the first ranks above the
  second"? Shape (a) ranks this purpose over every other purpose; shape (b) ranks it over one.
- **Q2**. Rows 6 to 9, the list "It never ranks over". I send the two scope items (rows 6 and 9)
  nowhere, on the argument that a scope bounds every purpose by its kind. I keep the owner's part
  (rows 7 and 8) in the ranking. Those goals, and `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`,
  are analysed in other themes: if one of them is ruled no goal, its item in the ranking falls with
  it. No word of the owner in the milestone document states this list.
- **Q2a**. Row 9, second half: "an ask that a design decision adds, or that a quality tool taken in
  brings, ranks as the decision that states it". I send it nowhere, on the argument that
  `primer@goals-bind` already gives a decision the rank of a presumption, below every goal. No goal
  then says it, and the analysis of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` has no
  row for it. Do you want the sentence kept in a goal, in the ranking or in that scope goal, or is
  the primer's rule enough?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments`: The documentation records the design, with the intent and the arguments behind it

**Kind**: purpose. Removed, the registers of decisions, of designs not kept, of what is outstanding
and of the evidence that would flip a decision would have no reason left to exist.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). `goal@knowledge-architect@records-live-beside-the-code` merges
into it, as its qualifier (see that goal). The first mapping table has no row for it.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: round 2, `arg-31`, the owner's two examples: "with the
current enforced categories of entries, such as goals, design, rejected...". None on the threshold
between an entry and a comment, which is why rows 1 and 2 send it to a head.

| 6 | "It is met while the installed text requires each of these to be recorded in the home the installed text gives it:" | the measure | existing head: `design@agent-skills@knowledge-table-home` (verified: "The knowledge table maps each kind of statement to its one home", its rows shipped in the primer) |
| 7 | "each decision ... in an entry of its own, carrying its standing argument, naming its rivals in its body or in the entries of the designs not kept that it cites;" | the measure | existing head: `design@agent-skills@standing-argument-in-head` (title only: "A design head carries the standing argument"); the rivals are `design@agent-skills@rejected-citation-direction` (verified) |
| 8 | "for any other decision whose reason the code or the agent-facing text it shapes does not show, a reason beside that code or that text, and in its commit message;" | the measure | existing head: `design@agent-skills@a-head-is-owed-by-an-entry-test` (verified, as row 2) |
| 9 | "what is outstanding;" | the measure | existing head: `design@agent-skills@knowledge-table-home` (verified: its rows name an issue) |
| 10 | "that evidence." | the measure | existing head: `design@agent-skills@knowledge-table-home` (verified: its rows name a tripwire) |
| 11 | "Requires, for instance: a recorded decision names the alternative that lost and why it lost." | a case | nowhere: repeats the statement (row 1) |
| 12 | "Admits, for instance: a decision and the designs it did not keep recorded in separate documents." | a case | existing head: `design@check@rejected-register` (title only: the designs not kept are entries of a register of their own; it cites this goal) |
| 13 | "Rules out, for instance: a decision recorded with its conclusion alone, its argument left to be derived again." | a case | nowhere: repeats the statement (rows 1 and 5) |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **A project's documentation records its design, with the intent and the arguments behind it** (draft slug: design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments)
>
> In a target project, the documentation records what was decided and why. Each decision the
> project records in an entry of its own carries its intent, its argument and the alternatives that
> lost. The reason of any other decision, where its code or its agent-facing text does not show it,
> is recorded beside that code or that text and in the message of its commit. What is outstanding
> is recorded, and so is the evidence that would flip a decision. A later session can then tell
> what it may change, and what a change costs, without deriving the argument again or reversing a
> decision blind. The record lives in the project's repository, beside the code, in files and
> commit messages an agent reads and edits like any other, never in an external tracker.

The last sentence is what `goal@knowledge-architect@records-live-beside-the-code` brings: Q4.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q3**. The threshold "whose reason one comment at one site cannot carry" leaves the goal for the
  head that already argues it. An agent may then reverse that threshold on argument and report it.
  Is that intended?
- **Q3a**. With the threshold gone, the draft names two tiers, a decision recorded in an entry of
  its own and any other decision, and no longer says which decision is which. It does not ask an
  entry, an argument and the losing alternatives of every decision: the head the threshold goes to
  says "The tests keep the decisions recorded few". Is the draft's reach the one you intend, or do
  you want the threshold's words kept in the goal?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@structure-and-workflow-work-together`: The documentation structure and the workflow work together

**Kind**: no goal. It states how two parts of knowledge-architect relate, "Neither the structure
nor the workflow is designed without the other". Removed, nothing the project provides loses its
reason to exist: the structure and the workflow each keep the purpose another goal gives them. It
bounds nobody, and it ranks nothing. The one outcome inside it, one home for each kind of record,
is stated by `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`: "A recorded item keeps one
home".

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` for the
sentence on one home; the rest is held by the head of the root's goals home, whose last sentence
of its first paragraph reads "The documentation structure and the workflow are designed to work
together." That head is text "the owner rules like a goal", in the words of the goal-setting
skill's "Where the goals sit", so the rest does not leave the owner-ruled text: it stays in it,
though I find it is no goal: Q5a. The first mapping table has no row for it. Five heads cite it:
`design@agent-skills@a-reference-claims-a-revisit`, `design@agent-skills@undesigned-work-is-an-issue`,
`design@agent-skills@roadmap-orders-issues`, `design@agent-skills@no-progress-record` and
`design@knowledge-architect@workflow-findings-stay-here`. The one I read whole for this,
`design@knowledge-architect@workflow-findings-stay-here`, leans on the one-home sentence: "a second
home for what is outstanding, against" this goal. The one pass re-points the five to
`goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`, reading each first.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none. The rewrite must ask: Q5.

It is not sent nowhere: no text free to change on argument holds it. Q5a |

- **Q5**. Is "the structure and the workflow are designed together" a purpose you hold apart from

  providing without it.

### `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`: The documentation stays consistent with the code and with itself

**Kind**: purpose. It is the owner's example B: "a project keeping design documentation up to date
with code, in a project developed by LLM agents". Removed, the checker has no reason left to exist,
and neither have the reviews and the audits that read documents against code.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). `goal@check@document-rules-are-checked` and
`goal@check@documents-stay-current` merge into it. One sentence must survive the merge, because
four heads rest on it by quoting it: "a tree that passes the checker conforms". They are
`design@check@the-regime-has-no-opt-out`, `design@model@an-unresolved-reference-is-reported`,
`design@model@a-wrong-declaration-is-a-finding` and `design@model@a-failed-parse-is-loud` (each
verified). The first mapping table has no row for this goal; its row for
`goal@check@document-rules-are-checked` names it as the goal merged into.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: round 2, `arg-31`, example B. None on the line between what
the tools check and what the reviews judge: Q6.

The exception is read against by `design@check@the-regime-has-no-opt-out`: Q11a |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **A project's documentation stays consistent with its code and with itself** (draft slug: documentation-stays-consistent)
>
> In a target project, agents change code faster than a review alone can follow. Reviews and audits
> judge what the documents mean: whether the code departs from what they state, and whether two
> documents agree. The checker and the commands that list the recorded items, as knowledge-architect
> ships them, support them and judge no meaning, whatever a project's own checks may do. A project
> declares to the checker what it records, where each kind of entry lives, and the kinds and the
> places by which one entry points at another, beside the rules of its own documents. In the texts
> the checker reads, a tree the checker passes meets every rule the checker holds and every rule
> the project declares, save those the project switches off by a declaration, with nothing cited
> missing. Whether a tree meets them is established by running the checker, not by a reviewer
> remembering the rules. A mechanical rule, such as that a flag a document names is one the binary
> declares, judges no meaning.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q6**. "The checker judges no meaning" stays in the goal, with one example. It reads as a scope
  that names a design it excludes, a check that judges meaning. No head argues it, and no word of
  the owner in the milestone document states it. Do you hold it as part of this goal, or is it a
  decision to argue in a head, which an agent may then reverse on argument?
- **Q6a**. Rows 5 and 11, and row 6 of `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`: that
  the listing commands "list and display the entries a review must read". The head that names the
  commands, `design@knowledge-architect@query-crate`, decides only that they are a crate of their
  own, and takes what they are for from two goals of `query`, ruled in another theme. If those two
  goals are ruled no goal, no text argues what the commands give a review. Do you hold that as part
  of a goal, or does it go to an issue of kind `design` until a head argues it?
- **Q7**. Row 9, the retrospective's question on how the reviews and the audits fared, is not in
  the installed text. I send it to an issue of kind `design`. Do you want it kept as work to
  schedule, or dropped?
- **Q7a**. Row 7. The goal asks, before every merge, a review that reads the documents the branch
  touches "and those that describe the code it touches, against the code and against each other".
  The head I cite covers the statements about code the diff writes, and no head argues the two
  other parts: the documents outside the diff that describe the code touched, and two documents
  read against each other. I send those two parts to an issue of kind `design`. Do you want them
  kept as work to schedule, or dropped?

>
> In a target project, each recorded item, one of a kind the project declares included, has one
> home and a name. It is cited by that name at each place where it bears on other work and where a
> project may write a checked reference, in a reference that says what it names, with one reading,
> and that the checker checks. A session working at such a place meets the item, and a session
> changing the item can list what to read again.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q8**. Your example A names one purpose: "a documentation model and checker that allow declaring
  various types of entries and citing them". The drafts split it: declaring kinds is in
  `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`, citing is here. Do you want example A
  as one goal of its own instead, with this goal's reach, "the item reaches the work it bears on",
  kept beside it?
- **Q9**. Rows 7 to 9 and 12 name a reading and two review questions. The installed text holds the
  reading in part: the reversal check, and the primer's reading of the record of code before a
  change. It holds neither review question, and not the retrospective's question. The parts it
  does not hold leave the goal for the issue that records them, which says of the reading only
  that it was not read against the installed text. The statement no longer asks for a review: the
  drift goal's statement and that issue carry it. Is that the reach you intend?

### `goal@check@documents-stay-current`: In a project whose gates run the checker on each commit, its references, its goals and the entries of its record, and the texts the checker generates stay correct at every commit

**Kind**: no goal. Reworded as an outcome of the project as a whole, it says that references
resolve and entries have their shape, which `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`
states, plus a question of when, "at every commit", which the standard leaves to design. Removed,
nothing loses its reason to exist: the checker keeps the reason that goal gives it.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` for its two
items that goal already states; every other sentence is held by an existing head. The first mapping
table says "head". I confirm that it is no goal, and I overturn "head" in one respect: no new head
is owed, because `design@check@a-commit-message-is-a-document` and
`design@check@generated-files-are-pure` already argue what it adds. Three heads cite it:
`design@git@repository-variables-dropped`, `design@model@anchor-names-unique` and
`design@knowledge-architect@checker-asks-git-through-the-crate` (each verified). Each leans on "a
run judges the project's own tree" or "each [reference] resolve"; the one pass re-points them to
`goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`, reading each first. Two issue entries
cite it too, as `cargo klarch show` lists them:
`issue@model@an-ignored-path-with-no-slash-passes-by-build-state` and
`issue@model@the-walk-does-not-report-what-the-per-clone-exclude-file-removes`; the one pass
re-points them the same way.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none on this goal. Round 2, #arg-26, on refining goals in
general, with its hedges: "If a goal refines another one, it is most likely that the goal is trying
to have the refined goal enforced by giving a more detailed reading of the refined goal in the
context of the component. Then, it is actually a design decision of what is done in this component,
whose argument stands on trying to follow the refined goal. It is probably not deserving the status
of goal." The owner hedged it in round 3, #arg-46: "About my statement on refinement: what I said
might not be true of all cases. I expect goals that refine another to be a shape grounded on the
refined goal. This might not always be the case though."

**Kind**: no goal. It bounds the cost of one operation, "a few mechanical edits", which is a
question of how much. It names parts that exist by a design choice: a Component, and the place
where a kind of entry lives. Its second sentence rests on a Component's goals, which the new
standard removes: "a change of purpose of the part it moves, which the goals carry". Under goals at
the root only, a Component's purpose is the statement of a design head, so that sentence is false
as written. Removed, nothing loses its reason to exist: the anchored reference grammar keeps the
reason `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` gives it.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head, and no new head is proposed. Two existing heads hold
the mechanism, `design@check@every-path-names-its-anchor` and
`design@model@register-home-reference`. Both take the bound from this goal and neither argues it,
so the bound itself, the definition of a relocation and the exemption of an adoption are a design
issue until the owner rules Q12. The first mapping table says "stands alone by its
title: purpose; whether it stays a goal is open". I overturn "purpose", for the reasons above. The
question the discussion left open is put to the owner: Q12.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none in the milestone document on whether it stays a goal.
`issue@model@a-reference-to-an-anchor-s-own-directory-breaks-when-the-anchor-moves` records a
ruling: "The owner ruled that the goal admits no exception for a reference naming an anchor's own
directory." It does not quote the owner.

**Head proposed**: none. A head `a-relocation-needs-only-mechanical-edits` fails the brief's test
of a rival and a fact of its own:

  `design@check@every-path-names-its-anchor` cites, and that head already states the fact:
  "relocating an anchor edits its one line of the project's `knowledge-architect.toml` and no
  document";
- its rival "a register's home cited by its file path" is `rejected@model@path-to-either-home-shape`,
  which `design@model@register-home-reference` cites, and that head already argues "a home that
  outgrows one file dangles every `path` to it";
- what would remain is the goal's bound restated. A derivation of it exists, from
  `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`: a recorded item is cited where it bears,
  so a citation must survive a move of what it names, or the reach is lost until each citation is
  repaired by judgement. Its rival is a relocation repaired reference by reference, from the list
  of dangling references the checker gives. I have no fact that defeats that rival: it weighs a
  count of edits, which no goal ranks. So the premise is a design issue, and Q12 puts its shapes.

Seven heads cite the goal: `design@check@goals-home-cites-upward-only`,
`design@check@every-path-names-its-anchor`, `design@model@anchors-are-components-and-locations`,
`design@model@register-home-reference`, `design@model@path-to-a-home-is-reported`,
`design@model@harness-kinds-cited-without-anchor` and `design@model@declarable-directory-register`.
Four issue entries cite it, as `cargo klarch show` lists them:
`issue@check@texts-citing-the-relocation-goal-state-its-old-measure`,
`issue@model@a-reference-to-an-anchor-s-own-directory-breaks-when-the-anchor-moves`, whose "Why it
matters" stands on "A recorded decision conflicts with a goal",
`issue@check@an-extension-cannot-see-which-register-a-document-belongs-to` and
`issue@knowledge-architect@no-command-renames-or-moves-an-entry-or-a-component`. The one pass reads
each of the eleven and re-points it as Q12 is ruled. Between two heads, `primer@goals-bind` ranks
nothing.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q12**. Does this goal stay a goal? I find it is none, and I propose no new head, since the two
  heads above already hold the mechanism and its rivals. What no text argues is the premise, that a
  relocation must cost mechanical edits alone, with the definition of a relocation (row 1) and the
  exemption of an adoption (rows 4 and 10). Three shapes:
  (a) you hold "a project can reorganise its documents at the cost of mechanical edits alone" as a
  purpose: say for whom, and I draft it as a root goal without the clause on a Component's goals;
  (b) it is a decision: `design@check@every-path-names-its-anchor` and
  `design@model@register-home-reference` gain the premise as a statement derived from
  `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`, a new member argued under the design
  skill, and the citing heads re-point to them;
  (c) it waits: one issue of kind `design` holds the premise, the definition and the exemption
  until a head argues them.
  My default is (b) for the premise, with rows 1, 4, 10 and 11 in the issue of (c).
- **Q13**. The question the slice's audit added. If it becomes a head, is your ruling, no exception
  for a reference naming an anchor's own directory, kept as a statement of
  `design@check@every-path-names-its-anchor`? Kept, it contradicts that head's sentence that such a
  reference "a move is expected to break", and the head is rewritten or the checker gains a
  spelling. Dropped, the issue on it closes with no change.
- **Q14**. Rows 2 and 6, the rule that a project relocates only on two occasions, go to an issue of
  kind `design`. No installed text holds the rule today. Do you want it kept as work, or dropped?

### `goal@check@checks-a-commit-from-its-hook`: The checker judges a commit's tree from the git pre-commit hook

**Kind**: no goal. It names one means, git's pre-commit hook, and a fixed block of cases, the forms
of `git commit` it covers. Its measure is a list of tests. It answers a question of when for
`goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`: before the commit exists. Removed,
`check --staged` keeps the reason `design@check@staged-tree-source` gives it: "It is the only tree a
session cannot judge before the commit exists".

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head. `design@git@index-file-variable-kept` exists and
holds the mechanism, but it does not hold the capability: it takes it from this goal. Its argument
against its rival reads "Refusing to run with it set leaves a pre-commit hook unable to run the
checker at all ... against" this goal. With the goal gone, that head must gain the statement that
the checker is to run from a pre-commit hook, and why: a new member, not an existing statement.
I confirm the row of the first mapping table, "head", and add that no new head is owed, only that
member: Q16a. Two heads cite the goal, `design@git@index-file-variable-kept` and
`design@git@repository-variables-dropped` (each verified), and one issue entry,
`issue@agent-skills@a-command-chained-after-a-failing-check-recurs`, as `cargo klarch show` lists
them. Re-pointing them to `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` does not hold
them: the draft of that goal says nothing of a hook or of "before the commit exists". The one pass
re-points them as Q16a is ruled.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "A project can run the checker from its git pre-commit hook, the hook git runs before it records a commit, to judge the tree that commit records, before the commit exists." | the capability | design issue. `design@git@index-file-variable-kept` (verified; its title ends "so a pre-commit hook judges the tree its commit records") holds how the hook reads the right index, and takes the capability from this goal. No head argues that the checker is to run from a pre-commit hook: `design@check@staged-tree-source` (verified) gives the motive of `check --staged`, "a partial commit", and names no hook. Q16a |
| 2 | "The commits this covers are those of `git commit`, `git commit -a` and `git commit <paths>`, the paths with or without `--` before them, given its message by `-m`, by `-F` or in the editor, with or without `-q`, and with no other option of `git commit`." | the covered forms | nowhere: held by `path@testing@tests/binary.rs`, the test `check_staged_as_a_pre_commit_hook_judges_the_index_the_commit_records` (verified: it runs the three forms, `-m`, `-F`, `GIT_EDITOR`, `-q` and `--`). The head measures the three forms; the options are in the test alone. Shown to the owner: Q16 |
| 3 | "Each is covered in the repository's main worktree and in a linked one." | the covered worktrees | existing head: `design@git@index-file-variable-kept` (verified: "in a linked worktree, the absolute path of that worktree's index or its lock") |
| 4 | "A target project is covered here when a `.git` is at or above its directory." | a bound | existing head: `design@git@repository-variables-dropped` (verified: it rests the bound on "the ask of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` that a target project's `.git` sit at or above its directory", which that goal states: "with its `.git` at or above the project's directory"). Its last sentence states the bound as what this goal "covers": the one pass rewrites that sentence to rest on the ask alone |
| 5 | "It is met while the checker's tests show, for each of the three forms, ... a commit whose tree fails, which the hook refuses, and one whose tree conforms, which git records." | the measure | nowhere: held by `path@testing@tests/binary.rs`, the same test, whose doc comment says "it is the measure of" this goal |
| 6 | "It refines `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`." | a refinement | nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@skills/knowledge-architect-goal-setting/SKILL.md`, rule 8 |
| 7 | "Requires, for instance: `git commit -a -m` in a linked worktree, on a tree that fails, is refused by the hook." | a case | existing head: `design@git@index-file-variable-kept` (verified) |
| 8 | "Admits, for instance: a hook that also judges a commit made with `--amend`." | a case | nowhere: repeats the statement: it forbids no other form |
| 9 | "Rules out, for instance: a hook that refuses a covered commit whose tree conforms." | a case | nowhere: held by `path@testing@tests/binary.rs`, the same test (verified: "each form refuses a failing tree and passes a sound one") |

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q16**. Row 2: the list of covered forms of `git commit` leaves the goals and is held by a test
  alone, free to change on argument. Is that list a promise to target projects that you want stated
  where a user reads it, such as the README of `check`?
- **Q16a**. Row 1. No head argues that a project can run the checker from its pre-commit hook: the
  head on the hook takes it from this goal. Is "a project can judge a commit's tree before the
  commit exists, from its hook" (a) an outcome you hold, to state in a root goal, or (b) a
  decision? Under (b), `design@git@index-file-variable-kept` gains the statement and its reason as
  a new member, argued under the design skill, and an issue of kind `design` holds it until then.

### `goal@model@a-tool-reads-the-documents-with-no-check`: Every tool of knowledge-architect that reads what a project's documents hold can read it through one library that judges none of what they say

**Kind**: no goal. Its subject is "any code of knowledge-architect", and "one library": it states
the purpose of the Component `model`, a part that exists by a design choice. Reworded as an outcome
of the project as a whole, nothing remains: no target project and no user is served by it. It is
the statement of `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate` (verified: "A tool over the record needs
the model and at most phases 1 to 3, never the last phase").

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head, and the heads exist:
`design@knowledge-architect@model-crate`, `design@model@no-check-below-the-model` and
`design@model@resolver-outcome`. The first mapping table has no row: the milestone document says
"the discussion did not sort it". Three heads cite it, the two of `model` above and
`design@query@query-depends-on-no-check`; `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate` says "It serves"
this goal, and the one pass rewrites that sentence: Q17.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none on this goal. Round 2, #arg-26, and its hedge of round
3, #arg-46, as quoted under `goal@check@documents-stay-current`.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "A tool here is any code of knowledge-architect that reads what a project declares and what its documents hold, such as a command that shows or searches entries, a work list over them, a renderer of the documents, or a checker of their content." | a definition | existing head: `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate` (verified: it names `id`, `worklist`, `show`, a writer's gate) |
| 2 | "Through the library such a tool reads what the project declares, what its documents hold, the entries they define, and what each reference names or why it names nothing." | what the library gives | existing head: `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate` (verified: "holds the manifest, the walk, the parse, the entity table, phases 1 to 3 and the resolver") |
| 3 | "To judge what a document says is to report that it breaks a rule on its content, such as a section an entry owes." | a definition of the line | existing head: `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate` (verified: "Phases 1 to 3 say whether the model can be trusted, not whether the content conforms", and where the line runs) |
| 4 | "It refines `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`, and reaches past it to tools no session task asks for." | a refinement, and a claim of wider reach | nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@skills/knowledge-architect-goal-setting/SKILL.md`, rule 8. "reaches past it" has no reason beside it and no head; shown to the owner: Q17 |
| 5 | "It is met while the library's public interface gives each of those readings with no such report attached and holds no function that makes one, and its dependencies hold no part of knowledge-architect that makes one." | the measure | in two parts. "with no such report attached and holds no function that makes one": existing head: `design@model@resolver-outcome` (verified: the resolver answers "with no finding attached", and "this crate writes none"). "its dependencies hold no part ... that makes one": existing head: `design@model@no-check-below-the-model` (verified: "This crate depends on no code that judges a document's content", with the command that shows it) |
| 6 | "Requires, for instance: a tool asks what a reference names, and gets the target or the cause of its failure." | a case | existing head: `design@model@resolver-outcome` (verified: "returns a `Target`, or a `Cause`") |
| 7 | "Admits, for instance: a tool that scans a document's parse again for a subject of its own, or that reads a file past the library." | a case | nowhere: repeats the statement: it forbids neither, and no head holds the case. `design@check@an-extension-builds-its-own-model` (verified) is on another subject: a project's extension that scans the parse of `check` through the public API of `check`. It says nothing of a tool of knowledge-architect over the library, nor of reading a file past it |
| 8 | "Rules out, for instance: the library depending on the checks of a document's content." | a case | existing head: `design@model@no-check-below-the-model` (verified) |

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q17**. With the three goals of `model` gone, `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate` cites one
  root goal, `goal@knowledge-architect@documents-render-as-a-linked-site`, and the goals the tools
  over the record serve, which another theme rules. Row 4, "reaches past it to tools no session
  task asks for", is sent nowhere. Do you hold a purpose that other programs, not only
  knowledge-architect's own, read a project's documents through a published library? If yes, it is
  a root goal to draft; if no, the row is dropped.

### `goal@model@a-tool-knows-the-read-is-right`: A tool can tell whether what the library read of a project is right

**Kind**: no goal. Its subject is "the library" and "a tool" of knowledge-architect, a part that

mapping table has no row for it. It is cited by `design@knowledge-architect@model-crate`,
`design@query@a-function-takes-a-configured-manifest`, one issue of `model`, and a doc comment at
`path@model@src/entity.rs`, line 1958, on `definition_findings`; the one pass re-points each to
`design@check@phases-gate-the-report`.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none.

| 2 | "An answer computed over it is unreliable." | why | existing head: `design@check@phases-gate-the-report` (verified: "every finding computed from that model afterwards is then unreliable in both directions") |

Three of the four: nowhere: held by `path@testing@tests/model_over_mocks.rs`, through the library's `foundation`: the test `every_phase_two_assertion_has_a_planted_defect_and_nothing_else_is_reported` names a home that is missing ("carries no tripwire home") and a file that "could not be read as text", and `a_slug_defined_twice_in_one_instance_is_reported_at_both_sites` names an entry defined twice. Two more tests, in `path@testing@tests/binary.rs`, show a missing home and an unreadable file through the binary, not through the library's public interface: `anchors_marks_a_missing_home_and_exits_one` and `an_unreadable_file_outside_the_walk_is_a_phase_two_finding_naming_it`. choice. Without the root goal it means nothing: its own body says "This is what lets an item be
cited where it bears, with one home."

mapping table says "stands alone: purpose". I overturn the row: the purpose is real, it is the
citing half of the owner's example A, and the root goal already states it. A second goal with the
same words is no second intent under rule 5. Nothing cites this goal but the milestone document, at
two lines, as `cargo klarch show` lists them.

checker that allow declaring various types of entries and citing them". Round 4, on the table: "I
agree on your list for the refinements."

### Decided

- An Admits case that the reworded statement still admits, and that no head holds, is sent
  `nowhere: repeats the statement`. Ground: the goal-setting skill, "What it admits, it neither
  asks for nor forbids", so the case binds nothing and nothing is lost. The owner sees each such
  row: `goal@knowledge-architect@records-live-beside-the-code` row 8,
  `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` row 15,
  `goal@check@projects-add-their-own-checks` row 7, `goal@check@checks-a-commit-from-its-hook` row 8,
  `goal@model@a-tool-reads-the-documents-with-no-check` row 7,
  `goal@model@recorded-items-can-be-referenced` row 6.
- A line "It refines" is sent nowhere, held by rule 8 of the goal-setting skill. Ground: the
  milestone document, "No goal holds a line 'It refines'".
- A sentence that a recorded issue of kind `todo` already holds as undecided work is sent
  `design issue`, naming that entry, and no second entry is proposed. Ground:
  `primer@where-knowledge-goes`, one home.
- Where two heads argue one sentence, the row names one as its destination and the other beside
  it. Ground: the brief asks for exactly one destination. Where a head holds one part of a sentence
  and no head holds the rest, the row is in parts, each with its one destination.
- No new head is proposed on relocation. Ground: the brief, "If you cannot name a rival and a fact,
  the destination is `design issue`, not a head": the rivals and the facts are those of
  `design@check@every-path-names-its-anchor` and `design@model@register-home-reference`, and I have
  no fact against the rival of the premise. The owner rules its shape in Q12.
- A measure sentence that says how the goal is judged in use, and that no head holds, is sent
  `nowhere: repeats the statement`: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift` row 12.
  Ground: the sentence is the statement, said of its failure.
- The questions are labelled `Q1` to `Q17` within this file. A question added after the
  verification of this theme takes the label of the nearest question before it, with a letter:
  Q2a, Q3a, Q5a, Q6a, Q7a, Q11a and Q16a.
  `design@agent-skills@ruled-items-labelled` lists no prefix for a file of
  `path@knowledge-architect@docs/goal-reviews/`, as the slice's audit subjects say.

### Met outside the task

- `cargo klarch show goal@check@checks-a-commit-from-its-hook` does not list the doc comment at
  `path@testing@tests/binary.rs`, line 4255, which cites that goal. The one pass must find such

  may hold it, which I did not read.
- `design@agent-skills@plan-read-against-the-record` quotes the drift goal's measure with "a

- `design@check@goals-home-cites-upward-only` argues from "A goal refines a goal of its own

  milestone document's table lists it for slice 2.
- `issue@agent-skills@the-review-does-not-ask-whether-items-are-cited-where-they-bear` opens on

  mapping table sorts as "head". The entry is re-read with that goal's ruling.
- The retrospective skill's source holds no question on how the reviews and the audits fared,
  though two root goals name one in their measures: recorded as Q7 and in the rows that send it to
  an issue.
- The Write tool refused the analysis file as a report file of a subagent. The brief's instruction

## Theme: The workflow for agents

Questions Q18 to Q47.

Branch `goals/standard-and-rewrite`, tree at the commit of the design audit of this slice. 13 goals: 4 of the root's goals home,
`register@knowledge-architect@goal`, and the 9 of `register@agent-skills@goal`. Nothing here is ruled. Every
draft is marked "draft, not ruled".

Conventions of this file:

- "The milestone document" is `milestone@plans@goals-at-the-root`. "The first
  mapping table" and "the second mapping table" are its two tables under "Mapping tables".
- A question to the owner carries a label `Q<n>`, numbered over this file.
- A row quotes a long sentence by its first and last words.
- A line "It refines ..." takes the destination `nowhere: held by
  crates/agent-skills/fragments/goal-standard.md, rule 8` ("It refines no other goal"), which
  removes the line. The row says where the relation it stated survives.
- A head named as "existing head" was opened and read, unless the row says "by its title".

Commands that take the facts again: `cargo klarch show goal@<anchor>@<slug>` for the citers of a
goal; `grep -n '^### ' crates/agent-skills/docs/design/*.md docs/design.md` for the heads.

`path@agent-skills@content/skills/`, the 16 agents and the primer are what it provides.

for it (it refines none). It takes in: the statement of `goal@agent-skills@no-contradiction-in-workflow`,
the list of `goal@agent-skills@one-skill-per-activity`, one clause of
`goal@agent-skills@installed-text-works-in-any-environment`, and the scope of
`goal@agent-skills@only-needed-instructions` (see each). It loses its measure, its cases, the
steps after each colon of its list, and its paragraph on continuous integration, which becomes a
head.

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **AI agents get a workflow that covers each generic activity of a target project that knowledge-architect's owner has taken in, and raises the quality and the efficiency of their work** (draft slug: agents-get-a-complete-workflow)
>
> A generic activity is one whose procedure has a part that does not depend on what the project
> builds; that part is its generic part. The workflow covers the generic part of each activity
> the owner has taken in, an activity that only some target projects carry out included. The
> activities include these, and the list grows:
>
> - setting up a project;
> - setting its goals;
> - discussing designs;
> - planning;
> - recording decisions;
> - tracking open issues;
> - developing;
> - working on a branch under a linear history;
> - releasing;
> - reviewing;
> - auditing a project on one aspect;
> - maintaining the agent configuration;
> - the retrospective;
> - the intake of a retrospective's findings.
>
> The workflow is complete: an agent carries each activity out with the workflow and what its
> project adds, and needs no instruction from outside them. An agent can follow it as written:
> each instruction says what it requires plainly enough to follow without guessing what it means,
> and no two that meet at one moment are ones a session cannot both obey, a precedence the
> workflow states between two counting as obeying both. It does not try to cover every case: what
> an agent's judgement handles well enough gets no instruction. It does not teach how to write
> Rust idiomatically, whatever the test of a generic part finds.

**Head proposed**: `installed-text-requires-no-ci-and-no-pull-request`, in
`register@agent-skills@design`, subject "The configuration a project holds".

- Title as a decision: "The installed text requires neither continuous integration nor pull
  requests, and assumes either only inside an offered shape a project takes or leaves whole".
- Goal cited: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`. Constraint derived: the
  workflow covers the generic part of an activity, and whether a project runs continuous
  integration or pull requests is particular to it.
- Nearest rival: the installed text assumes both, as it does today at the sites
  `issue@agent-skills@installed-text-assumes-pull-requests-and-ci` lists (the primer's coined
  terms, the planning skill, the design skill, the setup skill, the review skill).
- Fact that defeats it: the owner's report that in a private project with one contributor
  continuous integration on pull requests spends limited minutes and a pull request brings little
  value; and the four conditions of a target project name no hosting service.
- Entry test passed: test 2, the same reason respected at more than one site. The sites are the
  five texts above.
- Note: the head is written before the installed text conforms. The issue stays open and names
  the divergence, per `primer@intent-and-claims`.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q18.** The definition of "generic part" is used by this goal and by
  `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`. The entry shape says a
  definition several goals share goes in the home's head. Does it move to the head, or stay here
  with the other goal restating it in its own words?
- **Q19.** Row 3. Do you require that an activity only some target projects carry out is covered,
  or is that left to your choice when you take a part in?
- **Q20.** "Offered shape" leaves this goal with rows 16 to 20. It is used by
  `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`,
  `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped` and
  `goal@knowledge-architect@setup-brings-quality-tools`. If any of those keeps the word, its
  definition must go to the home's head instead of the new head. Which?
- **Q21.** Row 20 becomes a head that binds as a presumption. An agent may then argue the installed
  text into assuming pull requests, and report it. Do you accept that, or is "no installed
  instruction requires continuous integration or a pull request" a scope you hold as a goal?
- **Q22.** The draft's last sentence keeps the exclusion of Rust idiom and drops "a mechanical gate
  may ship" (row 11). Is the exclusion still yours, in these words?
- **Q22a.** Row 21. The draft keeps "plainly enough to follow without guessing what it means" word
  for word. `rejected@agent-skills@two-readings-left-to-judgement` lost to that sentence, and says
  it "is the candidate if the owner restates the goal ... so that an instruction whose readings
  all lead to acceptable acts meets it". Do you keep the sentence as it is, or restate it so?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-components`: The workflow leads a target project to be split into Components, each with one purpose

**Kind**: purpose. Removed, the component-creation skill and
`path@agent-skills@fragments/when-to-split.md` have no reason left. It passes rule 2 only if
"Component" is read as something the project provides to a target project, defined and then
named. The owner's wider wording, "modular units", passes without that reading.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). The row of the first mapping table, "stays a goal: the owner
leans to keeping it; a wider wording is drafted in the review", is confirmed. The draft below is
the wider wording, from the owner's words.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

- Round 4 (#arg-57): "It could be a mean to
  `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code` I think (and maybe others), because
  a modular project often leads to a clearer API, and design docs that are easier to act on and to
  follow. But I lean to keeping it as a goal. It is partially orthogonal to better code, and it
  bears on more than just code." And: "Maybe it could be under a more general shape though,
  "Projects are split into modular units", which would bind at more layers than just components:
  splitting code of one component, as well as splitting files of design docs by subject in one
  components, are places where splitting matters too and makes exploring the project and working
  in it easier overall. It also makes it easier for a human to review changes done by agents,
  which is one point that also matters to me."
- Round 1 (#arg-5): "this clashes strongly with the idea that the
  split in components is design, what is split, why and when is supposed to be left to design."
- Round 9, of an intermediate Component: "For now, stay silent on that."

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

The definition itself is held by `path@agent-skills@content/PRIMER.md`, section "The project's documents and how they are checked". That head cites this goal for the definition ("defines a Component as what the checker's manifest declares"); see Q27a |

Two heads lean on it: `design@agent-skills@dependency-decision-home` and the head of row 1 ("that each Component's design stays small enough to read whole") |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **The workflow leads a target project to be split into modular units, each with one purpose** (draft slug: projects-are-split-into-modular-units)
>
> A target project is split into units at each layer where a split helps: its parts, the code of
> one part, the documents of its design by subject. Each unit serves one purpose, so that an agent
> reads one unit whole and changes one part at a time, and a person reviews an agent's change more
> easily. One purpose per unit is a direction, not a rule a unit fails alone: what makes a split
> good is learned from use.

If the owner keeps the narrower form, the draft is the same with "Components" for "modular units",
its first sentence reading "A Component is a part of a project that the project declares: a
directory, or the project's root", and rows 1, 2 and 4 stay in the goal.

- **Q26.** The session said in round 4 that the wider form "competes with
  `only-needed-instructions`-style economy wherever a split adds files to read. That may call for
  a ranking." You did not answer. Where a split adds files to read, which comes first?

  `design@agent-skills@component-crate-mapping` refuse a Component that is a module directory

  rests three points on this goal's text: that an intermediate Component's own files hold no Rust
  code, "per" this goal; the defeat of its second rival, on the goal's definition of a Component;

  chose "to keep the goal as it stands". The wider draft holds neither the definition nor the
  sentence on an intermediate Component. Do both stay in the goal (the narrower form), or does the
  head take them as its own statements, the two defeats attributed to your choice?

  the plan documents. Keep the old slug instead?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`: The workflow improves through real use

**Kind**: purpose, with a scope the owner now doubts. Removed, the retrospective, its two files,
the intake and the route of a workflow file to knowledge-architect have no reason left. Its
second paragraph and its measure state the scope: no mock run, and no instruction on behaviour
without evidence from real use. Both are argued in heads already.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded), thinner. The first mapping table has no row for it. The
purpose stays. The admission rule and the rule on mock runs go to the two heads that argue them,
where they bind as a presumption. That answers the owner's words below: an argument that an
addition leads to a better result can then defeat the rule, which a goal does not permit.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

  too large. If taken literaly, it would forbid pretty much any change to the project. If you have

  what gets implemented or recorded in a target project), I think the addition should stand. If
  you have doubts, an issue is fine."
- Round 9: "If the usage of that actually surfaces in a consumer, we can learn from it and observe
  whether it works well or not, and why."

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **The workflow improves through real use** (draft slug: the-workflow-improves-through-real-use)
>
> Real use is the test of how an agent behaves under the workflow: a session that applies it
> inside a real target project, on work meant to merge into that project's main branch, under the
> supervision of a person who contributes to the project or owns it. What real use shows unclear,
> missing or wrong in the workflow reaches knowledge-architect and changes the workflow. A change
> it leads to that is an owner's to rule is ruled by knowledge-architect's owner.

  argument may reverse it and report it to you. Do you accept that, or is "no mock task and no

  `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`, which sends to knowledge-architect's owner a
  change of an ask and the taking in of a generic part, and not a change of the workflow?
- **Q31.** Rows 7 and 13. Is "an installed text that fails a goal is repaired on reading, with no
  evidence from use" a ruling of yours? If yes, it enters

  later, in an issue.
- **Q32.** Your words on D3 read as a rule of that head: an addition stands where the session
  argues that it leads to a better result in what a target project implements or records. Do you
  want the head reworded so, in the one pass?

### `goal@agent-skills@one-skill-per-activity`: Every activity of a target project whose generic part knowledge-architect's owner has taken in has one installed skill

**Kind**: no goal. It states how the installed text is cut into units. A skill is a part whose
existence comes from a design choice. Reworded as an outcome of the project, "agents get a
workflow that covers each activity", it is the complete-workflow goal. Nothing the project
provides would lose its reason to exist without it that does not lose it without that goal.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head, new, with its list of activities merged into
`goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`. The row of the first mapping table,
"head", is confirmed.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none on this goal by name. Round 4, on the table that
sorts it as a head: "I agree on your list for the refinements."

That line holds no "a sub-activity included": those words go to the new head `goal@agent-skills@one-skill-per-activity`, with the test of row 2 |
| 2 | "Two tasks whose methodologies differ ... are not bundled in one skill." | the test of two activities | new head: `goal@agent-skills@one-skill-per-activity`. `design@agent-skills@goal-set-review-in-goal-setting` records it as the owner's argument: "an activity is its methodology, not the scope of what it reads" |

shipped set" (beside `design@agent-skills@naming-rule`, which cites it).

- Title as a decision: "Each activity the workflow covers has one installed skill, cut by
  methodology, from which an agent carries the activity out".
- Goal cited: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`. Constraint derived: an
  agent that meets an activity finds every instruction of it without knowing where else to look.
- Nearest rival: skills cut by another unit, by what they read or by the artifact they write, or
  one skill holding two activities.
- Facts that defeat it: a skill loads whole, so a skill that holds two activities loads the
  procedure of one into every session of the other, the fact
  `design@agent-skills@component-skill-delivery` records against writing the Component procedure
  into the design skill ("would load it into every design discussion, most of which create no
  Component"); and a skill cut by what it reads bundles two methodologies, the fact
  `design@agent-skills@goal-set-review-in-goal-setting` records against the goal review as an
  audit axis.
- Entry test passed: test 2. Six heads lean on it: `design@agent-skills@naming-rule`,
  `design@agent-skills@audit-is-an-activity`, `design@agent-skills@component-skill-delivery`,
  `design@agent-skills@goal-set-review-in-goal-setting`, `design@agent-skills@admission-rules-home`
  and `design@agent-skills@intake-skill-home`.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q33.** Row 19 rules out a session loading another installed skill on its own judgement. As a
  head it binds as a presumption. Is that a ruling of yours to attribute, or the drafter's case?
- **Q34.** The merged list gains "maintaining the agent configuration" and "the intake of a
  retrospective's findings". Both have a skill. Do you confirm both as taken in?
