### `goal@agent-skills@no-contradiction-in-workflow`: No two installed instructions contradict

**Kind**: no goal of its own. Its statement is a sentence of the complete-workflow goal already:
"No two instructions are ones a session cannot both obey." It holds no intent without that goal:
by rule 5 it is a qualifier of the purpose it bounds.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`. This
overturns the row of the first mapping table, "head". The reason: a head would restate a sentence
the root goal keeps, and ten texts cite this goal as a ground that binds outright
(`design@agent-skills@expectation-set-bounds-scope`: a contradiction "is always in scope, because"
of it). Re-pointed to the root goal, each keeps that ground. As a head it would bind as a
presumption, which those heads do not assume. Two clauses of it that no head holds, on a stated
precedence and on "at one moment", join the merged sentence (rows 2, 3 and 7).

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none on this goal by name. Round 4, on the table: "I agree
on your list for the refinements." The table said "head", so the merge needs the owner's word.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "The installed text, every part of it, gives no two instructions a session cannot both obey." | the statement | stays in the goal it merges into (row 22 of the complete-workflow goal) |
| 2 | "A precedence an installed text states between the two leaves the session the move the prevailing one asks, which counts as obeying both." | that a stated precedence is no contradiction | stays in the goal it merges into, as one clause of the draft. No head states the rule (`grep -n -i precedence crates/agent-skills/docs/design/*.md` finds none that does). `design@agent-skills@questions-go-to-their-authority` holds one application, the primer's rank sentence, and cites this goal for the rule: "with it no session meets two instructions it cannot both obey, as `goal@agent-skills@no-contradiction-in-workflow` asks"; see Q35a |
| 3 | "It is met while every pair of installed instructions that meet at one moment leaves a session a move that obeys both, the move a precedence names counting as one." | measure; adds "that meet at one moment" | stays in the goal it merges into, for the words "that meet at one moment", in the draft; the rest repeats the statement (rows 1 and 2). No head and no installed text holds "at one moment": `design@agent-skills@workflow-axis-lenses` says "Two standards that no single session obeys together are no contradiction", a premise of its lens L8 on a review standard and an audit standard, which is narrower: two instructions one session holds at two moments are outside it; see Q35b |
| 4 | "A pair found in use that leaves none shows the goal unmet in practice." | the statement, negated | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 5 | "It refines `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`." | refinement | nowhere: held by `path@agent-skills@fragments/goal-standard.md`, rule 8. The merge keeps the relation |
| 6 | "Requires, for instance: where one skill says to commit and another says not to commit while a check fails, the installed text leaves a session a move that obeys both." | case | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 7 | "Admits, for instance: two instructions that would conflict if they met, which never meet at one moment." | case | nowhere: repeats the statement (row 3, whose words "that meet at one moment" stay in the draft); see Q35b |
| 8 | "Rules out, for instance: two installed skills that, at one review, ask for opposite outcomes of the same finding, with no precedence between them." | case | nowhere: repeats the statement |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard**: the sentence "no two that meet at one moment are
ones a session cannot both obey, a precedence the workflow states between two counting as obeying
both" of the draft of `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow` (draft, not
ruled).

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q35.** Merged into the root goal, against the table's "head"?
- **Q35a.** Row 2. Does "a precedence the workflow states between two counts as obeying both" stay
  in the goal, as the draft has it? Without it the sentence reads as ruling out every stated
  precedence. The other shape: the clause leaves the goal, and an issue holds it until a head
  argues it.
- **Q35b.** Rows 3 and 7. Does "that meet at one moment" stay in the goal, as the draft has it?
  Without it, two instructions that never meet count as a contradiction. The other shape is the

held to different criteria are two instructions a session cannot both obey. Nothing would lose
its reason to exist without it.

skills cannot produce an addition that another of them would refuse or reshape".

**Head proposed**: under the slug of `goal@agent-skills@reviews-judge-by-the-writer-s-criteria`, in `register@agent-skills@design`,
subject "Reviews".

- Title as a decision: "A review or an audit judges a text by the criteria that bind its writer at
  the time of the review, and by no other".
- Goal cited: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`. Constraint derived: no
  two instructions are ones a session cannot both obey, and a reviewer's criterion that the writer
  was not bound by is one the writer could not have met.
- Nearest rival: a reviewer with a standard of its own, stricter than the writer's or of the
  reviewer's preference.
- Fact that defeats it: a finding raised under a criterion that does not bind the writer has no
  repair the writer's instructions give, so each round raises it again;
  `design@agent-skills@behaviour-additions-need-real-use` already counts "divergent verdicts that
  a review standard and an audit standard give over one text" as a defect provable by reading.

  reading by direction, "since" this goal "asks that the criteria of the reviews and of the audits
  agree"), and the `%%` comment of `path@agent-skills@content/agents/spec-first-reviewer.md`.

**Questions for the owner**: none beyond the fate. The list of criteria (rows 2 to 6) enters the
head as its members, not as your ruling.

### `goal@agent-skills@installed-text-works-in-every-target-project`: An installed text works unchanged in every target project that installs it, one that holds none of knowledge-architect's goals and record included

**Kind**: no goal of its own; a qualifier of a scope. Its first clause is a sentence of
`goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` in the same words ("The installed
text names no path or convention particular to one target project, and may rely on what
knowledge-architect asks"). What it adds keeps its meaning with the design removed: the workflow
works in a project that holds nothing of knowledge-architect's own record.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`, of
another theme, as one sentence. This overturns the row of the first mapping table, "head". The
reason: 13 texts cite it, in 5 Components (`agent-skills`, `cli`, `model`, `query`, and the
root's issues) and in `path@agent-skills@CLAUDE.md`, as what an installed instruction must meet
outright. As a head, a session could argue an installed instruction into needing this
repository's record. The mechanisms it names are in heads already.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none on this goal by name. Round 4, on the table: "I agree
on your list for the refinements." The table said "head", so the merge needs the owner's word.

### `goal@agent-skills@only-needed-instructions`: The installed text holds only the instructions the workflow needs, and leaves to the session's judgement the cases it handles well enough

**Kind**: scope, not ranking. It bounds what the complete-workflow purpose covers, and names the
designs it excludes: an instruction for a case judgement handles well enough, and a fixed
sequence, count or mapping the work does not need. It ranks no two purposes: economy of the
installed text is no purpose of the project. By rule 5 a scope that bounds one purpose is a
qualifier of that purpose.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`, as two
sentences of its draft. This overturns the row of the first mapping table, "stands alone:
ranking", on the reason above. Its paragraph on structure goes to the head that argues it, where
it falls from a goal's weight to a presumption (Q39a); where that clause applies and where it does
not is held by no head (rows 6 and 7, Q39b). Its
paragraph on an act a goal's text states goes to an issue: it is the premise the slice's document
says expires.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none in the milestone document on this goal. The session
deferred its wording in round 7 (#arg-94), and the owner did not
answer by name. The rewrite must ask the owner.

The merged draft sentence holds no clause on structure, so the exclusion falls to a presumption; `design@agent-skills@structure-the-flow` and `design@agent-skills@workflow-axis-lenses` cite the goal for it too; see Q39a |

mapping table reads "head by its title; whether it stays a goal is open". I answer the open

### `goal@agent-skills@session-orchestrates-the-work`: The main session of a piece of work does itself what needs it, and hands the rest to agents it dispatches

**Kind**: purpose, on the owner's word; the open question of the audit subjects stays open (Q44).
Applying rule 1: removed, the dispatch of the record search, the form of a searcher's report and
the rule on how a dispatched conclusion is checked have no reason left. Seven passages of
`path@agent-skills@docs/design/record-search.md` cite it as their only goal. Against it, the
session's argument of round 2: "dispatching agents is a means. The outcomes are that the
session's context goes to design with you, and that you are told each decision." The owner
answered that the goal is orthogonal to the one it refined, and stated it with its "so that".
The draft keeps both halves in the owner's words.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded), in the root's goals home, in three sentences. The row of the
first mapping table, "stands alone", is confirmed. Its paragraphs on what a coherent part is and
on the cost of a check are what the owner left "to argumented design": the built part is in
existing heads of the record search, no new head is written, and the part not built stays in the
issue that already names it.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

- Round 1 (#arg-3): "The goal is that the main session orchestrates
  subagents to carry the work, so that it can focus on deciding design with the human
  interlocutor, and on reporting any decision taken. Nothing more detailed. When exactly these
  dispatch happen, and on which kind or size of work, is left to argumented design."
- Round 3 (#arg-47): "For the orchestration goal, i find it weird that
  it refines `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`. They are pretty much
  orthogonal IMO."
- The session, round 5 (#arg-67), on its 11 cases: "None changes the
  direction; each closes a question the rewrite leaves to a head."

"The dispatch issue" below is
`issue@agent-skills@the-installed-text-has-the-main-session-carry-work-it-could-dispatch`. It is
of kind `todo`. It holds the owner's leads on when a dispatch is worth it, and says of each that
it is "argued again where it is built".

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "The main session is the session that the person supervising the work exchanges with." | a definition | stays in the goal |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **The main session orchestrates agents that carry the work, and keeps its attention for the design and the decisions** (draft slug: session-orchestrates-the-work)
>
> The main session is the session that the person supervising the work exchanges with. It hands
> the work to agents it dispatches, and acts on what comes back. It can then give its attention to
> deciding the design with that person, and to reporting each decision taken.

**Head proposed**: none. The head `dispatched-conclusions-are-checked-as-the-session-s-own` is
withdrawn, for three reasons:

- For the record search, the one dispatch where the rule is built,
  `design@agent-skills@session-acts-on-the-verdicts` already states the decision, in its title
  ("checked as it checks its own claims") and its body ("no more and no less"), with its rival.
- What the proposal added is rows 14 to 17: the definition of a stricter check, the chain, the
  review and the bound on the cost. None is built, and no installed text states one, so a design
  home, which holds built intent, cannot take them.
- The facts it named defeat a stricter check ("no more"). None was named against a laxer check
  ("no less").

Rows 14 to 17 stay in the dispatch issue, as leads to argue where the dispatch is built.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q44.** A purpose or a means? The draft states orchestration as the outcome and the attention
  it frees as its reason, in your words of round 1. Read as a purpose, a harness with a far larger
  context still owes the dispatch. Read as a means, the goal is "the main session's attention goes
  to the design and the decisions", and dispatch is a head. Which do you hold?
- **Q45.** Row 17. The dispatch issue records "these checks should not raise the total cost
  significantly" as a lead of yours, not a ruling, with your hedge that you find it "hard to
  measure". The row follows that record: it stays in the issue. Do you rule it now as a cost, the
  hedge kept? If yes, it enters `design@agent-skills@session-acts-on-the-verdicts` attributed to
  you.
- **Q45a.** Row 13. The heads of the record search cite this goal for "checks a dispatched
  conclusion as it checks its own claims, no more and no less". The draft does not hold it. The
  dispatch issue records your view behind it as a lead: you hold "the main session as reliable as
  any dispatched agent", so its check of a dispatched agent's work "is neither stronger nor
  weaker than its check of its own claims". Is it a clause of the goal, a ruling of yours that
  `design@agent-skills@session-acts-on-the-verdicts` attributes to you, or does that head carry
  it on its own argument? The default: the third.
- **Q46.** Rows 3b, 4b, 4c, 8 to 12, 14 to 20 and 23 to 28 go to an issue of kind `todo`, which is reworded to
  hold them as leads to argue, not as rulings. Each was approved by you as goal text. Do you want
  any of them kept as a ruling, attributed to you, in a head once the dispatch is built?
- **Q46a.** Rows 2, 3b and 4c. The draft follows your words of round 1 and "Nothing more
  detailed": it holds neither "It does itself what needs it", nor "the goals that it discusses
  with that person", nor "the questions put to that person". Do you want the goals or the
  questions named in the goal, beside the design and the decisions?
- **Q47.** The goal comes to the root's goals home with its slug. In which theme file?

### Texts above the first goal

**`register@knowledge-architect@goal`.** Its first paragraph states what the project is for. Two of its sentences
belong to this theme: "It gives AI agents a workflow that covers each activity of a target project
whose generic part knowledge-architect's owner has taken in, development included, and aims to
raise the quality and the efficiency of their work and of the code they write", and "The
documentation structure and the workflow are designed to work together". The first stays in the
head, which the owner rules like a goal. The second is of another theme. Its list of terms opens
"These words hold in these senses for the goals of this home and of every Component": "and of
every Component" leaves, since no Component states a goal. The definition "the workflow, or the
installed text" stays: every goal of this theme uses one of the two words. If Q18 and Q20 are
answered so, "generic part" and "offered shape" join the list.

**`register@agent-skills@goal`.** One sentence: "The agent skills are the text
knowledge-architect installs in a target project for its agents: the workflow that the root's
goals name." The home keeps no entry. The sentence states a Component's purpose, which is the
statement of the head that argues its existence: `design@knowledge-architect@installed-text-crate`
(by its title: "The installed text lives in its own crate, which the crate holding the
installed-file check depends on"). Until slice 2 the file stays with its title and one line saying
that the goals are in the root's goals home.

  that already argues the shape.

  issue" and named that issue, not a new one. Ground: `primer@verify-before-relying`, "Check
  whether it is already known".
- Three rows of the first mapping table are overturned on "head" in favour of a merge

## Theme: The owner and the agents

Questions Q48 to Q58.

Four goals of the root's goals home, `register@knowledge-architect@goal`, and the text above
its first goal. Read at the commit "[plans] The design audit of the first slice of the goals
milestone" (the branch's head when this file was written). "The milestone document" is
`milestone@plans@goals-at-the-root`. No goal of this theme holds a line "It refines", so the first
mapping table has no row for any of them. Questions carry a label `Q<n>`, numbered through this
file.

Heads named below were opened and read. Three are in
`path@agent-skills@docs/design/project-configuration.md`
(`design@agent-skills@questions-go-to-their-authority`, `design@agent-skills@owner-word-holds-as-far-as-its-premise`,
`design@agent-skills@decisions-reported-to-the-owner`), five in `path@agent-skills@docs/design/owner-and-intent.md`
(`design@agent-skills@owner-rules-at-a-fork`, `design@agent-skills@intent-through-goals-not-overrule`,
`design@agent-skills@derived-decision-proceeds-on-default`, `design@agent-skills@default-ruled-before-merge`, `design@agent-skills@fork-choice-in-weak-form`),
one in `path@agent-skills@docs/design/design-skill-and-heads.md` (`design@agent-skills@settled-means-recorded`).

### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`: The owner decides what the argument from the goals, the record, the code and the sources leaves open, every action that cannot be undone, and, as knowledge-architect's owner, each change of an ask and the taking in of a generic part, and is informed of every decision the agents make that earns durable text or that the owner could have ruled

**Kind**: purpose. It states what the project provides to the owner of a target project: authority
over what no argument settles, and sight of what the agents decided. Without it, the routing of a
question to its authority, the "Decided" section of a turn and the rule that a default is ruled
before the merge would have no reason left to exist: the purpose of autonomy without drift alone
asks for none of them.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). The goal holds one intent, as its own body says ("The two
halves are one intent"). Its body is mostly design that existing heads already argue: the exact
list of what goes to the owner, the way the owner changes a settled decision, and the forms of the
report. Those sentences go to their heads, save three points that no head read holds and that go
to a design issue (rows 13, 17, 23 and 27). Two members of its list, a change of an ask and the
taking in of a generic part, speak of knowledge-architect's own owner. They stay in this goal: the
theme that analyses `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` and
`goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s` sends their sentences on these two
members to this goal, so this goal is their one holder (Q49).
`goal@agent-skills@goals-state-the-owner-s-intent` refines this goal; that
goal belongs to another theme, and its fate does not change this one. The goal is cited at 66
lines of the tree, 39 of them in design homes (`cargo klarch show`), the most of the theme.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

- Round 2, #arg-27: "Overall, the point I want to make clear is that I
  feel there is no need for the project owner to take an arbitrary decision in the project." and
  "They are not supposed to be about overruling on individual decisions. In practice, the only
  point where it makes sense to rule on a decision is by how costs are weighted." and "If I judge a
  cost too large, i can overrule a decision without needing a goal." and "This is the part where
  the owner word should enter design (along with a few mores such as facts), and I think this part
  is already well handled by design, which asks me about facts and cost weightings."
- Round 2, #arg-28: "It improves agent autonomy, and it should not in
  practice result in problems, because in practice this decision can only be reversed on a better
  argument."
- Round 2, #arg-29: "If another shape has costs, the workflow has them
  go through me AFAIK. If it has no costs, I have nothing to say against that." The session
  corrected its premise in #arg-35, and the milestone document notes:
  "The owner did not answer it."
- Round 2, #arg-30: "And there is a last wall of reviews before merge,
  including transcript reviewers, which make sure that significant decision reversals are not
  happening behind my back anyway."
- Not the owner's words, but the decided design that reads them, "A goal is a purpose, a scope or a
  ranking": "The owner's word enters design through the weighing of a cost at a fork and through
  facts the owner holds, among a few more such that the owner did not list, not through a goal on
  one decision. A goal of the three kinds stays a way for the owner to change a decision, as
  `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` says."

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0a | Title: "The owner decides what the argument from the goals, the record, the code and the sources leaves open, every action that cannot be undone, ... and is informed of every decision the agents make that earns durable text or that the owner could have ruled" (without the clause of row 0b) | the goal's statement | stays in the goal |
| 0b | Title, the clause "and, as knowledge-architect's owner, each change of an ask and the taking in of a generic part" | two members that concern knowledge-architect's own owner | stays in the goal, in the body of the draft, with rows 10 and 11; see Q49 |
| 1 | "In a project that follows the workflow, this one included, its owner states the project's intent in its goals and brings the facts only the owner holds." | what the owner brings | stays in the goal |
| 2 | "Agents decide, by argument, what these settle: the goals, the record, the code and its measurements, and the sources the project follows, save the items the list below sends whatever the argument settles." | what agents decide | stays in the goal, its last clause as "save what this goal gives the owner whatever the argument settles" |
| 3 | "They report each such decision that earns durable text, as below." | announces rows 16 to 18 | nowhere: repeats the statement |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **The owner decides what no argument settles, and sees what the agents decided** (draft slug: the-owner-decides)
>
> In a project that follows the workflow, the owner states what the project is for and brings the
> facts only the owner holds. Agents decide, by argument, what the goals, the record, the code and
> the sources the project follows settle, save what this goal gives the owner whatever the argument
> settles. The owner decides the rest: a choice between shapes that all survive the argument, and a
> contradiction no argument resolves. Whatever the argument settles, every action that cannot be
> undone is the project's owner's, and so are, for knowledge-architect's own owner, each addition,
> tightening or removal of an ask that binds every target project, and whether and when
> knowledge-architect takes in a generic part. Each decision the agents make that earns durable
> text, and each choice they make that the owner could have ruled, reaches the owner, who can
> contest it. The two halves are one intent: agents decide only while the owner can see what they
> decided.

The draft takes one more sentence from the merge proposed for the fourth goal below, where its
whole body is shown.

**Head proposed**: none. A sentence that leaves has a head that already argues it or a text that
holds it, save rows 13, 17, 23 and 27. For those the analysis names no rival and no fact that
defeats it, so their destination is a design issue, with Q50a and Q50b.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q48.** The clause "through a goal, a fact or an argument, ... and never by a word that gives
  none" leaves the goal for `design@agent-skills@intent-through-goals-not-overrule`, which records
  it as the owner's proposal. The owner's words of round 2 read: "If I judge a cost too large, i
  can overrule a decision without needing a goal." Two readings. (a) A stated weighing of a cost is
  an argument, so the clause stands, and "a goal" in it means a goal of the three kinds; this is
  what the milestone document records. (b) The owner may overrule on cost a decision that the
  argument settled, with no fork: the head is then reversed and the draft's "the rest" grows. The
  analysis builds on (a). Does the owner confirm it?
- **Q49.** The two members on knowledge-architect's own owner (rows 0b, 10, 11, 15b) stay in this
  goal, with "whatever the argument settles". The ground: the theme "Whom the project serves, and
  adoption" sends the matching sentence of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` to
  this goal, and `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped` cites this
  goal for its own; had this theme sent them back, each theme would name the other as holder and
  the statement would be lost. The draft then names "an ask that binds every target project" and
  "a generic part", words that two other goals define. Does the owner want the two members here,
  or in those two goals, or in a head?
- **Q50.** The words "and nothing else" leave the goal with the list. The open defect
  `issue@agent-skills@texts-send-the-owner-more-than-the-closed-list` rests on them: it then names
  a conflict between texts and a head, which `primer@goals-bind` does not rank. Does the owner want
  the closure kept in the goal, as in "The owner decides the rest, and nothing more is sent for a
  ruling"?
- **Q50a.** "The durable text that records it is that report" (rows 17, 23 and 27) is held by no
  head: `design@agent-skills@decisions-reported-to-the-owner` rules the "Decided" section and
  records the commit messages as a rival that lost. The draft says only that such a decision
  "reaches the owner", which leaves open whether the durable text is enough or a line of a report
  is owed beside it. Does the owner hold "the durable text is the report" as intent, to keep in
  the goal, or is it a member for that head, to argue in a design issue?
- **Q50b.** "A proposal on what the argument settles, ... put before the owner for challenge inside
  an exchange, ... is not sent for a ruling" (row 13) is held by a head for a design discussion
  only. It matters only if the closure of the list is kept (Q50). If the owner keeps the closure in
  the goal, does this sentence stay beside it, or go to a head through a design issue?
- **Q51.** "The order of known work" is given to the owner by this goal (rows 5c and 25, the second
  a Requires case), and two other goals cite it for that
  (`goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s` and
  `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`). No design head found says a choice of
  order is sent to the owner (`design@agent-skills@roadmap-orders-issues` says only "in the order
  the owner wants the work done"), and the primer's knowledge table says only where the order is
  written. The theme of `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s` sends its own
  sentence on it to that same row of the primer, so after the one pass no goal and no head would
  state it. The analysis records it as a ruling of the owner to write into
  `design@agent-skills@roadmap-orders-issues`. Does the owner confirm that the order of known work
  is the owner's to choose, whatever the argument settles or as a fork?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-s-word-is-verified`: Agents verify the owner's word before acting on it, and build nothing on a contradiction

**Kind**: purpose. It states what the project provides to the owner: no work built on a premise of
the owner that the project's own evidence shows false. Without it, `primer@owner-word-premise` and
the hold on work that rests on a contradiction would have no reason left to exist. The test is
passed narrowly: the grounding half of `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts`
covers a proposal of the owner inside a design exchange, and this goal covers a word of the owner
in every activity.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). Its second paragraph, on the premises of a goal, is a mechanism
that names commit messages; it leaves the goal. For what the premises of a goal are, and their
check when the goal is written or reworded (rows 3 and 4a), no head holds it and no installed text
states it (a search for "premise" in the goal-setting skill's source, the goal reviewer's, the
goal-standard fragment and the design subdocument on goals finds nothing), so it goes to an issue
of kind `design`. What happens when work meets evidence against a premise of a goal (rows 4b and
7) is held by `design@agent-skills@owner-word-holds-as-far-as-its-premise`, a goal being a ruling
on intent as that head defines one.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: None. The rewrite of this goal must ask the owner.

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled; no word of the owner in the
milestone document bears on it, so the one pass asks the owner before it writes this):

> **Agents verify the owner's word before acting on it, and build nothing on a contradiction** (draft slug: the-owner-s-word-is-verified)
>
> An agent takes no word of the owner as granted. Before acting on a word other than a goal, it
> checks what the word assumes against the project's goals, its record, its code and the sources it
> follows. A contradiction that no source or argument resolves goes to the owner, and nothing is
> built on it until the owner answers.

**Head proposed**: none. Rows 3, 4a and the first half of row 9 would pass entry test 2 (a reason
to respect at several sites: the goal-setting skill and every skill that acts on a goal), but the
analysis cannot name the fact that defeats the nearest rival, "a goal's premises are checked like
those of any other word, before each act on it". The destination is the issue.

- **Q53a.** The draft keeps "other than a goal" (row 2). It then states no check of a goal's

  the draft would require a goal's premises to be checked before each act on the goal, the rival
  the analysis cannot defeat. Does the owner want the exception kept in the goal, or the check of a
  goal's premises stated in it, and with which occasions?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts`: The owner designs with agents as counterparts

**Kind**: purpose. It states what the project provides to the owner: a design exchange in which
both sides propose and each challenges the other. Without it, the discussion form of the design
skill, and the rule that a choice left open meets the owner before it reaches the main branch,
would have no reason left to exist: every thread could close on the agent's argument alone, which
is the design `rejected@agent-skills@every-thread-closed-by-argument` records.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). Its first two paragraphs are the goal. The rest closes
questions of when ("before it reaches the main branch") and by what means (which acts count as an
exchange), which existing heads argue, save two points no head read holds (rows 9b and 11b).

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

- Round 2, #arg-27: "However, personally (and I believe anyone should probably follow this
  principle, at least I think we can ask that of target projects), when I develop a project (under
  knowledge-architect or not), I want to take the solution that under argumentation, will fulfill
  our goals the best, under acceptable costs."
- Round 2, #arg-32: "Because a design decision has to close the
  reading, the choice will be discussed and argued, several shapes might be tested, and a change of
  design can happen without a goal drafting session. This has high chances of improving the design
  in the long term IMO."
- Round 1, #arg-3, on another goal, names this exchange as an end: "so
  that it can focus on deciding design with the human interlocutor, and on reporting any decision
  taken."

### `goal@knowledge-architect@owner-attention-on-what-is-theirs`: The owner's attention is spent on what is the owner's to read or answer

**Kind**: no goal of its own. Applied as a purpose, the test of rule 1 finds nothing that loses
its reason to exist with it. Its list of what is the owner's (the proposals, their arguments, the
questions only the owner answers, the reports) is the sum of what
`goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts` and
`goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` give the owner. That a proposal comes "with its
grounding and its consequences already established" is the counterparts goal's "with its
consequences". That the agent carries the work is "with less supervision" in
`goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`. What is left is a cost, the owner's reading
time. It is used as a cost in the record: `design@agent-skills@search-runs-once-the-shapes-settle`
says a cost "strains" it, and `rejected@agent-skills@every-thread-closed-by-argument` and
`rejected@agent-skills@head-repaired-only-when-touched` keep a doubt on it. It is no scope: it
names no design it excludes. It is no ranking: it ranks no two purposes.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`, as one sentence: the
agents carry the investigation, the measurements and the tracking, so that what reaches the owner
is what is the owner's. By rule 5 it is a qualifier of that goal: "what is the owner's to read or
answer" has no meaning without the statement of what is the owner's. The cost of the merge: five
citing lines in design homes and two `rejected` entries cite this goal as a ground or as a strain
(`cargo klarch show`), and are re-pointed; where one leans on it as a binding rank of the owner's
time over another cost, that head is rewritten, and the weighing becomes a fork the owner rules.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**:

- Round 2, #arg-27: "I feel there is no need for the project owner to
  take an arbitrary decision in the project."
- Round 2, #arg-29: "If it has no costs, I have nothing to say against
  that."
- Round 2, the session, #arg-35, a cost put to the owner and not
  answered: "fewer and more abstract goals rank fewer costs, so more decisions become forks and
  reach you as cost weightings. That is the entry point you say you want, and it costs you more
  questions than today."

No word of the owner in the milestone document states the attention goal itself: the rewrite asks
the owner.

| # | sentence (verbatim, or its first and last words where it is long) | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "The agent carries the investigation, the measurements and the tracking of what is outstanding, so that the owner's attention goes to what is the owner's to read or answer:" | the statement | stays in the goal (the one it merges into) |
| 2 | "the proposals put before the owner;" | first member | nowhere: held by `register@knowledge-architect@goal`, in `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts` ("The owner meets each proposal of the exchange") |
| 3 | "their arguments;" | second member | nowhere: held by `register@knowledge-architect@goal`, in the same goal ("with its consequences, and its rival where one stands") |
| 4 | "the questions only the owner answers;" | third member | stays in the goal: it is the statement of the goal it merges into |
| 5 | "the reports of what agents decided." | fourth member | stays in the goal: it is the second half of the goal it merges into |
| 6a | "It is met while the installed text gives the agent each of those tasks," | measure | nowhere: repeats the statement (row 1) |
| 6b | "and puts a proposal before the owner with its grounding and its consequences already established." | measure | stays in the goal (the one it merges into), as "establish the grounding and the consequences of a proposal before it reaches the owner". `path@agent-skills@content/skills/design/SKILL.md` holds it for the one message of `skill@knowledge-architect-design@bounded-work` only ("what the grounding found, the proposal, its nearest rival and the fact that defeats it, the consequences the owner must know, and the default"), so no text holds it whole; see Q56a |
| 7 | "Requires, for instance: the agent takes the measurements a proposal rests on before it puts the proposal to the owner." | case | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 8 | "Admits, for instance: the owner reads the whole transcript of a session by choice." | case | nowhere: repeats the statement (it shows that the statement bounds what is asked of the owner, not what the owner may read) |
| 9 | "Rules out, for instance: a proposal put to the owner whose consequences the owner must work out." | case | nowhere: repeats the statement, as `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts` makes it |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled): the sentence joins the draft of
`goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`, whose whole body then reads:

> **The owner decides what no argument settles, and sees what the agents decided** (draft slug: the-owner-decides)
>
> In a project that follows the workflow, the owner states what the project is for and brings the
> facts only the owner holds. Agents decide, by argument, what the goals, the record, the code and
> the sources the project follows settle, save what this goal gives the owner whatever the argument
> settles. The owner decides the rest: a choice between shapes that all survive the argument, and a
> contradiction no argument resolves. Whatever the argument settles, every action that cannot be
> undone is the project's owner's, and so are, for knowledge-architect's own owner, each addition,
> tightening or removal of an ask that binds every target project, and whether and when
> knowledge-architect takes in a generic part. Each decision the agents make that earns durable
> text, and each choice they make that the owner could have ruled, reaches the owner, who can
> contest it. The two halves are one intent: agents decide only while the owner can see what they
> decided. The agents carry the investigation, the measurements and the tracking of what is
> outstanding, and establish the grounding and the consequences of a proposal before it reaches
> the owner, so that the owner's attention goes to what is the owner's to read or answer.

**Head proposed**: none.

**Questions for the owner**:

- **Q55.** Is the economy of the owner's attention a purpose the owner holds on its own, so that the
  goal stays, or a qualifier of what the owner decides, as proposed? If it stays, the analysis
  cannot name what the project provides that only it justifies; the owner would have to name it.
- **Q56.** If it merges, a head that cites it to put the owner's time above another cost loses a
  binding ground, and that weighing reaches the owner as a fork. The owner was told in round 2 that
  thinner goals cost "more questions than today" and did not answer. Is that cost accepted here?
- **Q56a.** The merged sentence keeps, from the goal's measure and its Requires case, that the
  grounding and the consequences of a proposal are established "before it reaches the owner" (rows
  6b and 7). No text holds that whole, so dropping it would lose it. It states an order between the
  agent's work and the owner's reading. Does the owner hold that order as intent, to keep in the
  goal, or as a question of when, to argue in a design issue?

Four goals analysed, four sections. No new head is proposed. Four issues of kind `design` are
proposed: on the premises of a goal (second goal, rows 3, 4a and 9); on the durable text as the
report (first goal, rows 17, 23 and 27; Q50a); on a proposal put for challenge that is not sent
for a ruling (first goal, row 13; Q50b); and on the goal-setting draft and the proposals inside an
exchange (third goal, rows 9b and 11b; Q54a). Sentences that goals of another theme also state:
rows 0b, 10, 11 and 15b of the first goal, with
`goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` and
`goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`; this goal keeps them (Q49).

the goal does not say). None of the three is in the list of such heads in the slice's spec.

### Texts above the first goal

The head of `register@knowledge-architect@goal`, lines 3 to 28. Under the new shape, the head
"states what the project is for as a whole, which the owner rules like a goal", and holds the
definitions the goals share.

### Decided

- The questions are labelled `Q48` to `Q58`, numbered through this file.
  `design@agent-skills@ruled-items-labelled` gives no prefix for a file of
  `path@knowledge-architect@docs/goal-reviews/`, as the slice's audit subjects note; the session
  that commits the analysis may give them another prefix. A question added by the correction of
  this section takes the label of the nearest question of its goal and a letter: Q50a, Q50b, Q53a,
  Q54a, Q56a.
- A row of a table is a sentence, or a clause of one where two clauses go to two destinations (rows
  0a and 0b, 5a to 5c, 15a and 15b of the first goal; 4a and 4b of the second; 9a and 9b, 11a and
  11b of the third; 6a and 6b of the fourth). Ground: the brief asks one destination per row.
- Where a sentence stays in the goal in part and its remainder is held by a head, the row says
  both. Ground: one destination for the whole sentence would hide the part that leaves.
- No new head is proposed for this theme. Ground: each sentence that leaves a goal is argued by a
  head that was read, or is held by a text, or cannot be argued by the analysis and goes to a
  design issue (the premises of a goal, the durable text as the report, a proposal put for
  challenge, the goal-setting draft and the proposals inside an exchange).
- The analysis builds on reading (a) of Q48, the one the milestone document records.
- The two members on knowledge-architect's own owner stay in
  `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`. Ground: the other theme names this goal as their
  holder, and one holder is needed; the owner rules it in Q49.
- A draft is shown as a quotation with a bold title and a draft slug, as the other themes of this
  file show theirs. Ground: a goal entry is a level-two heading, which a file of analysis must not
  hold.

## Theme: The tools

Questions Q59 to Q77.

Branch `goals/standard-and-rewrite`, read at the commit of the design audit of this slice. 17 goals in 9 goals homes: 3 of the
root, 3 of `cli`, 1 of `git`, 2 of `query`, 1 of `mutate`, 1 of `transcript`, 1 of `gates`, 3 of
`testing`, 2 of `xtask`. No goals home was edited.

**How to read the tables.** One row per sentence, or per clause where one sentence states two
things. "Other theme" marks a goal this analysis did not sort. A destination "stays in the goal"
that names a goal of another theme is a proposal to that theme, repeated under "Questions for the
owner".

**Two drafts carry the theme.** They are named G-A and G-B below, and each is drafted in its goal's
section:

- G-A, from `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`: knowledge-architect
  provides tools that do the work the workflow asks of agents, where a tool does it cheaper or
  better.
- G-B, from `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`:
  knowledge-architect holds the generic part of the tools and procedures that target projects set
  up.

### Words of the owner used below

Verbatim, from `milestone@plans@goals-at-the-root`, with the round. Each goal's section names the
labels that bear on it.

- **W1**, round 1: "Overall, I think that goals should be about outcomes of the project as a
  whole, and that this is currently not correctly reflected."
- **W2**, round 1, the owner's example being the git driver Component: "goals under sub components
  often tend to be about how the component fits inside the rest of the project. However, this
  clashes strongly with the idea that the split in components is design, what is split, why and
  when is supposed to be left to design."
- **W3**, round 1, the owner's example being the xtask maintenance crate: "This being a goal does
  seem weird to me. First, because its existence is mandated by the installed set. And second,
  because it is more like design to me, which stands on a clear argument: having one place that
  wraps around maintenance tasks, and having a single gate command, makes it far easier to work in
  the project, as well as for keeping the agent instruction set coherent."
- **W4**, round 2: "So goals are supposed to steer the project, its design and the work, toward
  something that provides a given service or result, with the detailed features and their API
  shapes left to discussion, often to maximize ergonomics, and sometimes with specific constraints
  on more precise outcomes. They are not supposed to be about overruling on individual decisions."
- **W5**, round 2: "The goal should not constrain that shape to an exact retrospective question.
  The existence of the retrospective skill and the list and content of its questions is design."
- **W6**, round 2: "If a goal refines another one, it is most likely that the goal is trying to
  have the refined goal enforced by giving a more detailed reading of the refined goal in the
  context of the component. Then, it is actually a design decision of what is done in this
  component, whose argument stands on trying to follow the refined goal. It is probably not
  deserving the status of goal." Hedged in round 3: "I expect goals that refine another to be a
  shape grounded on the refined goal. This might not always be the case though."
- **W7**, before the discussion, at the goal of `cli`, on a proposal to extend a test to every
  command for each kind of refused invocation: "tbh, the goal itself is probably enough. I don't
  think it is worth it to try everything to have a mechanical test that detects any breach of that
  goal. Keep the effort on that reasonable. Goals are supposed to cover aspects that shape the
  future of the project, and that argumentation alone would not necessarily be able to defend (for
  example the cost/effort being one day considered to high might end up outweighing the
  justification or value)." and "Here, I think the goals stand because even with a mechanical
  test, someone might judge the effort to have a clean cli api too high and just disable the
  test."
- **W8**, before the discussion, at the same goals: "Goals should not be added just for the sake
  of adding goals."
- **W9**, before the discussion, at the intake of forty retrospective files: "Since goals bind
  very strongly, I want to avoid having too much of them, especially when they are things that can
  rest on argument. Because that means a reversal would be a better shape. If a goal state it, it
  would never be questioned, thus never improve. I think goals should be overall focused more on
  outcomes of the project and not on its shape."
- **W10**, round 2, on the criterion W7 uses, which the session had named
  `a-goal-is-what-argument-cannot-defend`: "I am not fully in favor of this shape. I proposed it,
  but I am uncertain it is truly the right answer here." The thread was withdrawn.

**Rulings recorded outside the milestone document.** Several commit messages attribute rulings to
the owner on the goals of this theme. They are attributions, not the owner's words, and they are
not in the milestone document. They are named by the commit's subject where a row uses one:

- **C-git**: "[git] The git Component's goal states what an operation of the library is, on the
  owner's rulings".
- **C-cli-1**: "[cli] The goal the owner ruled: an invocation its arguments refuse writes nothing,
  and says the command did not run".
- **C-cli-2**: "[cli] The goals the owner ruled on what the command line is for: one program for

- **C-cli-3**: "[cli] Two cases the owner's rulings placed, written into the goals on the owner's

- **C-query**: "[query] The goals the owner ruled for `query`, `transcript` and `create`, and the

- **C-mutate**: "[review] Repairs of the review before the merge: two content losses closed, the

- **C-testing**: "[testing] The goals the owner ruled for `testing`, and the heads that cite them",

### `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`: A mechanical task the workflow requires of a session is done by a command or a library function of a published crate of knowledge-architect

**Kind**: purpose. Removed, the tools that take a task from a session would have no stated reason
to exist: `klarch id`, `klarch worklist --out` and `--verify`, `klarch transcript`, and the planned
commands that rewrite a branch's history. Five heads of `register@query@design`, one of
`register@transcript@design`, one of `register@mutate@design`, one of `register@check@design` and
two of `register@agent-skills@design` cite it.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded), as G-A. The first mapping table's row, "stands alone:
purpose", is confirmed: its intent holds without `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`,
since a workflow can be complete and still leave every mechanical step to a session. The rewording
widens it as `issue@knowledge-architect@the-mechanical-tasks-goal-omits-what-a-helper-does-better`
asks, and so takes in the outcome of both goals of `query` and of the goal of `transcript`. The
form of the tool, "a command or a library function of a published crate", leaves the goal: rule 2
calls the exact form of an interface a decision.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W4. In the milestone document, the session's sorting to
which the owner agreed in round 4: "I agree on your list for the refinements." The widening is the
owner's, recorded in the issue named above and not verbatim: the goal "does not cover an action
asked of an agent that no mechanical task performs, and that a command could make more efficient by
making it mechanical, or by a helper that performs complex logic to give a better result".

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1a | "Where the installed text requires of a session a task whose result follows, with no judgement, from the tree ... and that takes several steps or a script the session would write, ... so every session gets the same result without building it again." | the outcome: such a task is done by a tool | stays in the goal |
| 1b | in the same sentence: "a published crate of knowledge-architect offers a command that does it, or a library function that the installed text has the project's own tooling call" | the form of the tool | design issue: the rule of one program, described under `goal@cli@one-program-for-its-tools` below, which closes "a command, not a library function alone" and whose proposed head is withdrawn |
| 2 | "Rewriting a branch's history holds such tasks: rebasing it ...; applying a message ...; and folding a repair into the commit it repairs, with the check that no content was lost." | three instances | nowhere: held by `issue@knowledge-architect@no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history`, docs/open-issues/no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history.md, which lists the three. Its Summary names this goal as what names them, so the one pass rewords it to state the three itself |
| 3a | "Those three count as taken in;" | a fact the owner holds: the owner took the three in | owner's ruling, into `issue@knowledge-architect@no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history`: no head exists, and that issue is the work the ruling orders. The issue does not hold the ruling today: its only ground is this sentence, "The three tasks above are taken in already, by `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`". The one pass writes the ruling into it, attributed to the owner, Q60a |
| 3b | "any other such task is owed such a command or such a library function once knowledge-architect's owner takes its generic part in, per `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`." | the owner gates what is taken in | stays in the goal: `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` (other theme), whose title states "the taking in of a generic part" |
| 4 | "Taking a part in is an act of knowledge-architect's owner of its own: a task is not taken in because the installed text requires it." | the same gate | stays in the goal: `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` (other theme) |
| 5 | "When such a command or function is built follows the order of known work, which is knowledge-architect's owner's, per `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`." | a question of when | nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@knowledge-architect/PRIMER.md`, the knowledge table's row on docs/roadmap.md, "the order in which the owner wants known work done" |
| 6 | "It is met while each such task ... has such a command, or such a function ..., of a published crate of knowledge-architect." | the measure | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 7 | "A task taken in and found in real use with none shows the goal unmet in practice." | how use shows it unmet | existing head: `design@agent-skills@premortem-as-watch-points`, which holds the retrospective's question on a script or a task carried by hand, and names this goal as what it watches |
| 8 | "It refines `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`." | a refinement | nowhere: held by `path@agent-config@skills/knowledge-architect-goal-setting/SKILL.md`, rule 8: the line leaves, and the intent stands without the goal it names |
| 9 | "Requires, for instance: a command that folds a repair into the commit it repairs and checks that no content was lost." | an instance | nowhere: held by `issue@knowledge-architect@no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history`, reworded as row 2 says |
| 10 | "Admits, for instance: one task offered both as a command and as a library function." | a question of by what means | design issue: the rule of one program, as row 1b |
| 11 | "Rules out, for instance: installed text that has each session write its own script to reword a commit that is not the newest." | the statement, negated | nowhere: repeats the statement |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **knowledge-architect provides tools that do the work its workflow asks of agents, where a tool does it at a lower cost or with a better result**
>
> The workflow asks tasks of the agents of a target project. Where the result of a task follows
> with no judgement from what the project holds, the workflow's rules and what the session gives
> it, and the task takes several steps or a script the session would write, or where a tool would
> reach the result at a lower cost to the agent or reach a better one, knowledge-architect provides
> a tool that does it. So each session gets the same result, and none builds its own means again.
> Finding something in the project, in its recorded items, in its history or in what its past
> sessions said, is such a task.
>
> A tool is something knowledge-architect publishes for a person or an agent working in a target
> project to run, other than the text it installs.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q59. The widening: does G-A take "at a lower cost or with a better result", the two cases the
  issue records, or does the owner want a second goal for them, the issue's other lead?
- Q59a. The goal binds only a task "that takes several steps or a script the session would write",
  and names what its result follows from: "the tree, the repository's history, the workflow's
  rules and what the session gives it". The draft keeps both, the first two sources as "what the
  project holds". Without the condition on several steps, the goal would owe a tool for a task of
  one step. With the widening of Q59, "at a lower cost" may make the condition unneeded. Does the
  owner keep the condition in the goal?
- Q60. G-A names no gate by the owner ("once knowledge-architect's owner takes its generic part
  in"). The draft leaves the gate to `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`. Does the owner
  want it restated here?
- Q60a. "Those three count as taken in" leaves the goal, and
  `issue@knowledge-architect@no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history` holds that fact only
  by citing the goal. Does the owner confirm that the three tasks of rewriting a branch's history
  are taken in, so that the one pass records it in that issue as the owner's ruling?
- Q61. The draft's last paragraph defines "a tool" in the words of the head of the goals home of
  `cli`. Does the definition go in the goal, or in the head of the root's goals home?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`: The generic part of a tool or a procedure that a target project sets up or changes is shipped by knowledge-architect once knowledge-architect's owner takes it in

**Kind**: purpose. The head of the root's goals home names it the second of the project's two main
purposes. Removed, what the project ships that keeps no document would have no reason to exist: the
gates library, the hand mutation, the development skill.
`design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host`, `design@mutate@mutation-command` and
`design@agent-skills@development-skill` cite it as their ground.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded), as G-B, and `goal@knowledge-architect@setup-brings-quality-tools`
merges into it. The first mapping table has no row for it. Its second paragraph and its measure
hold a policy on the copy a target project keeps of a shipped part. That policy is a decision, and
no head and no installed text holds it.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W4 and W9. None names this goal. The rewrite of this goal
must ask the owner.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 2 | "The generic part of one is the part whose procedure does not depend on what the project builds, as `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow` defines the generic part of an activity: it serves each target project that carries out its activity, or is meant to." | a definition shared with a goal of another theme | stays in the goal; the one pass states the definition once, in the head of the root's goals home, since two goals name it |

Q64a |

No text but this goal and `goal@gates@gates-from-a-list` holds it: a search of the tracked Markdown, Rust and TOML files for "merges into the main branch" finds these two goals, and one other sentence of docs/goals.md, on when a branch merges |

**The design issue of rows 10 to 12, 13b, 16, 17b, 18 and 20 to 22.** What a target project does with its own
copy of a part once a released version ships that part. The goal admits keeping the copy after the
project has considered the shipped part. `goal@gates@gates-from-a-list` says the opposite of a
behaviour of a gate command: "the copy goes at that move". The two differ because the gates are an
ask of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` and another part is none; no text says
so. I searched the setup skill's source and the primer's for "own copy", "its copy" and "ships the
part" and found nothing, so no installed text delivers either rule. I cannot name the fact that
defeats the rival, a project that must drop every copy at its move, so this is an issue of kind
`design`, not a head. Rows 13b and 17b are the same policy for this repository: a part counts as
shipped here when it merges into the main branch, and a command of this repository that carries
out a shipped procedure is this repository's copy of it.

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled):

> **knowledge-architect holds the generic part of the tools and procedures that target projects set up**
>
> Work in one target project sets up or changes tools and procedures that make its development
> more efficient or the project better, such as a gate, a maintenance command, a kind of reviewer,
> an external survey, a testing method or a publishing method. The generic part of one is the part
> that does not depend on what the project builds. knowledge-architect ships that part once its
> owner takes it in, so that every target project gets it, and work on one target project makes
> each of the others more efficient. A target project that adopts knowledge-architect gets the
> tools that judge its quality among them, and has to design none of them itself. What is
> particular to one project stays in that project. So knowledge-architect is about more than
> keeping a project's documents.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q62. No word of the owner in the milestone document names this goal. Does the draft state the
  owner's intent? Its clause "work on one target project makes each of the others more efficient"
  is taken from the first paragraph of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`.
- Q62a. The goal's title and the head of the root's goals home state the owner's gate: "once
  knowledge-architect's owner takes it in", "and that knowledge-architect's owner takes in". The
  draft keeps it as "once its owner takes it in": without it, G-B would owe every generic part.
  Row 4 sends the rest of the gate to `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`. Does the owner
  want the gate stated in G-B, or left to that goal alone, as Q60 asks of G-A?
- Q63. The policy on a project's own copy leaves the goal for an issue of kind `design`. What is
  the owner's reason for letting a project keep its copy of a part once it has considered the
  shipped one, where a copy of a gate behaviour must go?
- Q63a. The first bullet of the measure asks that each tool or procedure a session sets up be
  reported to knowledge-architect's owner. It leaves with the measure, and
  `issue@knowledge-architect@the-retrospective-asks-for-no-tool-a-session-set-up`, of kind `todo`,
  stands on it. Does the owner still want that report? If so, the one pass rewrites the issue to
  stand on the owner's word and on G-B, and its moment stays a design question.
- Q64. Rows 4, 5 and 9 send a sentence to a goal of another theme. Does the owner agree that each
  of the three goals named holds it?
- Q64a. Row 6b: the three forms a part taken in ships as, "as installed text, as an offered shape,
  or as a command or a library of a published crate", fit no destination: they are means, and no
  head and no text states the three. Does the owner want them stated in one place, or is the form
  of each part left to the head that decides that part?

**Kind**: no goal of its own. It fails rule 5: its body defines itself by two other goals, "the

part knowledge-architect's owner has taken in, per
`goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`", so it means nothing without
them. What it adds is that the setup gives each tool "in a recommended shape", which is a means.
Removed, nothing loses its reason to exist while G-B says every target project gets the generic
part of a gate.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`,
G-B. The first mapping table has no row for it. `cargo klarch show` lists 10 citing lines outside
the milestone document: the setup skill's source, four heads of `register@agent-skills@design`,
`design@create@rust-scaffold`, `design@gates@gates-crate`, two issues and the goal of `gates`. Each
is re-pointed to G-B in the one pass.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W3, on the single gate command: "it is more like design to
me, which stands on a clear argument". None names this goal. The rewrite must ask the owner.

### `goal@cli@one-program-for-its-tools`: Every tool of knowledge-architect is a command of one program, unless its result does not exist without running the project's build or its tests

**Kind**: no goal. A Component states no goal, and reworded for the project, "a project gets every
tool by naming one package and pinning one version", it is no purpose: removed, every tool keeps
its reason to exist, and only how a project gets them changes. It is no scope either: it bounds
neither whom nor what the purposes serve. It is the shape of a delivery, which W4 leaves "to
discussion, often to maximize ergonomics".

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head, which cannot be argued yet: its rule goes to an
issue of kind `design`. The first
mapping table has no row for it. Four heads cite it as the reason a tool is or is not a command of
`klarch`: `design@knowledge-architect@cli-crate`, `design@knowledge-architect@transcript-crate`,
`design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host` and `design@gates@gates-crate`. One reason at
four sites earns a head by test 2 of `primer@design-heads`. I found no fact that defeats its
nearest rival, so the head first proposed, `one-program-for-the-tools`, is withdrawn, as the
paragraph below the table says. `design@knowledge-architect@the-binary-crate-keeps-the-package-name`
already holds part of the first sentence.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W4 and W7. C-cli-2 attributes to the owner that "the main
goal of the crate is bundling the project's crates into the binary, its maintenance tooling apart,
and concentrating its parsing", and that "the rule goes in the goal, the owner's pick over the
session's default of a decision per exception. The owner was unsure of the criterion". Under the
new standard the session's default returns: the rule is a decision.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1a | "A project gets those tools by naming one package and pinning its version," | how a project gets the program | existing head: `design@knowledge-architect@the-binary-crate-keeps-the-package-name`, "A project that runs the checker or writes an extension names this package alone of the checker's for what it builds", with `design@check@installed-binary-version-check` for the pin |
| 1b | in the same sentence: "whichever tools those are, a later one included." | the rule: every tool, a later one included, is a command of that program | design issue: the rule of one program, below |
| 2 | "A tool whose result does not exist without running the project's build or its tests stays out of that program, and the project's maintenance tooling may offer it, whether it chooses what to run or its caller does." | the exception and its criterion | owner's ruling, into `design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host`, the head that applies the exception today, "the tool's result does not exist without running the project's tests": by C-cli-2 the owner's first wording was "that a tool stays out if it runs the project's build or tests", and the session's default the owner agreed gave "whose result does not exist without running them" |
| 3 | "A command of that program picks neither itself; running a command its caller names on the invocation is no breach, where the command's result exists without it." | a reading of the exception | owner's ruling, into `design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host`: by C-cli-2, "a command given a test command by its caller is not running the tests, the owner's own reading" |
| 4 | "It is met while each other tool is a command of that program, and no command of it picks and runs the project's build or its tests, which a test of the program's commands shows of those it names." | the measure | design issue: the rule of one program, below. I did not find the test the measure names: a search of crates/testing/tests/binary.rs and of crates/cli/src/ for "project's tests", "the project's build" and "picks" finds no test |
| 5 | "Requires, for instance: a new tool that searches the record is a command of that program; so is one that reads the agent harness's session transcripts." | the rule, for two new tools | design issue: the rule of one program, below. `design@knowledge-architect@transcript-crate`, "`cli` depends on it, and offers the command", and `design@knowledge-architect@query-crate`, "`cli` depends on it", state the placements built today, the first per this goal; neither states the rule for a new tool |
| 6 | "Admits, for instance: a tool also offered as a library function; a library with no command of its own that the program links; a project's own command, in a program of its own; a task only this repository's own tooling performs, which is published to nobody; a command rewriting history that runs a command its caller names at each commit." | five limits of the rule | design issue: the rule of one program, below; the fifth is also held by `issue@knowledge-architect@no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history` |
| 7a | "Rules out, for instance: a second program a project must install to consult its record;" | the statement, negated | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 7b | "a command of that program that picks and runs the project's tests; a command that runs the project's tests because a setting names them, with no name on the invocation." | two cases of the exception | owner's ruling, into `design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host`: C-cli-3 records that the owner agreed the second case |

**Head withdrawn**: `one-program-for-the-tools`, first proposed for `register@knowledge-architect@design`,
which holds `design@knowledge-architect@cli-crate` and
`design@knowledge-architect@the-binary-crate-keeps-the-package-name`. **The design issue of rows
1b, 4, 5 and 6**, and of rows 1b and 10 of `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`,
is the rule it would have stated.

- The rule: "Every tool of knowledge-architect is a command of `klarch`, unless its result does not
  exist without running the project's build or its tests".
- Nearest rival: one program per tool, such as a binary of its own for the transcripts.
- The fact first given against the rival does not hold. It said that a second program needs a
  second pin and a second substitution in every installed instruction that names it. The source of
  the installed text holds two placeholders, `{{command}}` and `{{primer-import}}`, and the
  development skill's source names a second program literally, `cargo x mutate run`, with no
  substitution. `design@agent-skills@xtask-pins-checker` pins the checker, the gates library and
  the mutation library "all pinned exactly, at the version `[project] checker-version` declares":
  one pin for three packages. So the tree holds a counter-instance, and I can name no fact that
  defeats the rival.
- What stands without it: row 1a, held by
  `design@knowledge-architect@the-binary-crate-keeps-the-package-name`, and the owner's intent that
  C-cli-2 attributes, "the main goal of the crate is bundling the project's crates into the
  binary". By `primer@design-heads`, an intent of the owner that states an outcome is a goal to
  draft, and a pick at a fork is recorded in the weak form: Q67a.
- The exception has no argued reason either. Its rival is a `gates` command of `klarch`, and
  `rejected@gates@gates-command-of-the-checker` states its consequence, "It would make `klarch` run
  cargo, the linter and the tests", and no reason why that is refused. The head that holds the
  exception records it as the owner's choice in the weak form, with the owner's doubt on the
  criterion, unless the owner gives the reason, Q66.
- Entry tests a head on the rule would pass: test 1, a command line others consume, and test 2,
  one reason at four sites.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q66. What is the reason a command of `klarch` never picks and runs the project's build or its
  tests? The record states the consequence and no reason. Without one, the head that holds the
  exception records the owner's choice, "which no goal ranks".
- Q67. Does the owner agree that this is no goal? W7 held "the goals stand" for `cli` on the
  criterion W10 then doubted.
- Q67a. No fact I found defeats the rival of the rule, one program per tool. Is "a project gets
  every tool of knowledge-architect by naming one package and pinning one version" an outcome the
  owner wants of the project, to be drafted as a goal of the root? Or what is the reason for one
  program, on which a head would stand? Until the answer the rule is an issue of kind `design`,
  and the one pass re-points the four heads that cite this goal to what the answer gives.

### `goal@cli@one-home-for-the-command-line`: That program's command line is declared in one place, apart from what its tools do

**Kind**: no goal. It states where code sits among Components: "`cli` holds the declaration of
every command". Rule 1 says "a statement about one part of the project is design too". Reworded
for the project it has no outcome left: emptied of its design, the project has no "one place".
Removed, nothing the project provides loses its reason to exist.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head: the existing heads argue it whole. The first mapping
table has no row for it. `design@knowledge-architect@cli-crate` argues the crate against
`rejected@knowledge-architect@single-crate`, and `design@cli@command-parsing-home` argues the
parsing against `rejected@cli@each-crate-parses-its-commands`.
`design@agent-skills@top-level-binary-crate` holds the owner's argument: "grouping the parsing,
with the checks applied at parsing, is more convenient".

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W2. C-cli-2 attributes to the owner: "an invalid
invocation is the parser's and an invalid request the tool's, the owner's split".

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1a | "`cli` holds the declaration of every command of that program," | the placement | existing head: `design@cli@command-parsing-home` |
| 1b | in the same sentence: "and refuses an invalid invocation: arguments that are wrong whatever the project holds, which a check of the value alone can tell." | the check of a value alone, made while parsing | existing head: `design@agent-skills@top-level-binary-crate`: "That crate parses the arguments, applies the checks that can be made while parsing". `design@cli@command-parsing-home` cites that head, and itself speaks only of "A combination of arguments the domain refuses" |
| 2 | "Whether a value is valid in the project is an invalid request, which the tool answers, since finding out may take the tool's own logic; so is a value that names something in the project, or that the command is asked about, whatever its form." | the line between the parser and the tool | owner's ruling, into `design@cli@command-parsing-home`: the split is the owner's, and its second clause a default of the session the owner agreed, by C-cli-2 |
| 3 | "What a command does is mostly pulled from the crates of the tools, or assembled in `cli` from what they provide." | where the logic sits | existing head: `design@cli@command-parsing-home`: "Each command's logic is a typed function of the crate that holds it ..., or is assembled here from those functions" |
| 4 | "So a tool can change what it does with no change to how it is invoked." | the title's "apart from what its tools do" | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 5 | "It is met while no crate that program links, `cli` apart, declares a command's arguments, and a check of any other value that needs only the value is made while parsing, which a look at the declarations shows." | the measure | nowhere: repeats the statement; `issue@cli@some-value-checks-are-made-after-parsing` holds what is unmet |
| 6 | "Requires, for instance: a new command's arguments are declared in `cli`; a date an argument takes is checked while parsing; so is a regular expression an argument takes, and the form of the name of a project a command creates." | four placements | owner's ruling, into `design@cli@command-parsing-home`: C-cli-3 records the last two as placed by the owner's rulings |
| 7 | "Admits, for instance: a tool outside that program keeping its own parsing; a rule the tool's own type holds, which the declaration spells; a command in `cli` that composes two crates' functions; a crate that program links using a parser privately." | four limits | existing head: `design@cli@command-parsing-home`, which says "`gates` and `mutate` keep that shape" and "is assembled here from those functions", and whose reason, clap's major version kept out of the crates' interfaces, covers a parser used privately |
| 8 | "Rules out, for instance: a crate that program links deriving its own commands' arguments for `cli` to flatten." | the rival | existing head: `design@cli@command-parsing-home`, with `rejected@cli@each-crate-parses-its-commands` |

**Questions for the owner**

- Q68. `issue@cli@some-value-checks-are-made-after-parsing` and `path@cli@CLAUDE.md` cite this
  goal. After the one pass they cite `design@cli@command-parsing-home`, a presumption. Does the
  owner agree?
