### `goal@cli@a-refused-invocation-does-no-work`: An invocation of a command `cli` offers that its arguments refuse on the command line writes nothing, and its exit code says the command did not run

**Kind**: no goal. It is a property of one interface: no purpose, since nothing the project
provides loses its reason to exist without it; no scope; no ranking. The slice's spec lists it
among the questions put to the owner. W7 held it a goal because a later session "might judge the
effort to have a clean cli api too high". That is the criterion of the withdrawn thread, W10. Under
the three kinds a cost judged too high is weighed at a fork, by the owner, in a head.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head. The first mapping table has no row for it. Two heads
argue its second half on facts of their own: `design@cli@exit-code-ladder`, "Without code 2, a
mistyped invocation or an unreadable input is indistinguishable from a tree that fails its check",
and `design@cli@arguments-parse-through-clap`, "These binaries run blind, as gates, so an unknown
flag or an invalid combination has to be a refusal rather than a no-op". Its first half, that a
refusal by arguments writes nothing, is argued by no head: the heads cite the goal for it. It gets
a new head.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W7, W8, W10. C-cli-1 attributes six rulings to the owner,
quoted in the rows that carry them.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "This holds for every command `cli` offers, whichever commands those are." | the range | owner's ruling, into `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`: by C-cli-1, "every command `cli` offers is covered, by a rule that reaches a future command, with no list" |
| 2 | "A command a project adds beside them is the project's, and so is what a project's own program does with a refusal." | the limit of the range | owner's ruling, into `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`: a default of the session the owner agreed, by C-cli-1 |
| 3 | "An invocation is refused by its arguments when it gives an argument the command does not declare, omits one the command requires, gives a value the argument's declaration does not accept, or gives a combination the command does not accept." | the four kinds | owner's ruling, into `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`: "the session's closed list of four kinds", by C-cli-1 |
| 4 | "The refusal comes before the command writes anything, with an exit code that says the command could not run, which differs from each code that says it ran." | the decision | new head: `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`; the code is `design@cli@exit-code-ladder`'s |
| 5a | "A run that reads the code as a verdict, as a gate does, then never takes such an invocation for a check that passed," | the reason, for the code | existing head: `design@cli@arguments-parse-through-clap`: "These binaries run blind, as gates, so an unknown flag or an invalid combination has to be a refusal rather than a no-op". `design@cli@exit-code-ladder` argues code 2 against a tree "that fails its check", not against a check that passed |
| 5b | in the same sentence: "and the caller knows this refusal wrote nothing." | the reason, for the write | new head: `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`; no existing head says it |
| 6 | "It is met while each of those four kinds of refused invocation behaves so, which tests show on the commands they give it to, each seeing that exit code and nothing written." | the measure | nowhere: held by crates/testing/tests/binary.rs, `every_command_refuses_an_argument_it_does_not_declare` and `refused_with_an_untouched_tree` |
| 7 | "Requires, for instance: a new command `cli` offers, given an argument it does not declare, exits with that code and writes nothing." | an instance | nowhere: held by crates/testing/tests/binary.rs, `every_command_refuses_an_argument_it_does_not_declare`, whose comment says "over every command the declaration holds, a future one included" |
| 8 | "Admits, for instance: a read of the project before the refusal; a value the argument's declaration accepts and the command then refuses or answers negatively, by the state of the tree or by what the value names, whatever its code; a code of could-not-run after a write that failed, which is no refusal by arguments." | three limits | owner's ruling, into `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`: the second and the third are defaults of the session the owner agreed, by C-cli-1 |
| 9 | "Rules out, for instance: an unknown argument ignored, so that a gate passes on a check it did not run." | the rival | existing head: `design@cli@arguments-parse-through-clap` |

**Head proposed**: `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing`, in `register@cli@design`.

- Title: "An invocation of a command of `cli::Command` that its arguments refuse writes nothing".
- Goal it cites: none. It stands on a fact, as #arg-68 found a head
  may. If the owner wants a goal cited, the candidate is
  `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` (other theme), with the constraint "a
  check that did not run is never read as a check that passed".
- Nearest rival: a value or a combination checked where the command uses it, after the command
  began to write.
- The fact that defeats it: clap resolves the whole command line before a command is dispatched,
  so refusing first costs nothing at the parse, as `design@cli@exit-code-ladder` says of the code.
  The rival leaves a caller that runs blind with a changed tree behind a code that says the command
  never ran. The record holds no instance of the rival. C-cli-1 corrected two comments that said
  exit 2 promises an untouched tree, "which `worklist --out` and `install-agent-skills` do not
  keep; they now say which command they hold of": the behaviour stays, and the goal admits it, "a
  code of could-not-run after a write that failed, which is no refusal by arguments".
- Entry tests: test 1, the contract of a command line that gates and extension binaries consume,
  and test 2, one reason respected at every command.
- The owner's words on the test effort, W7, are carried into the head as the owner's weighing of a
  cost, with their hedges: the statement is "probably enough", the owner does not "think it is
  worth it to try everything to have a mechanical test that detects any breach", and the effort
  on it stays "reasonable".

**Questions for the owner**

- Q69. The spec's question: does this goal stay a goal? My judgement is that it is no purpose, no
  scope and no ranking. If the owner holds that a clean command line is something the project
  exists to provide, it is a purpose of the project, and it is then drafted for the whole command
  line and not for the refusal alone.
- Q70. A citation of this goal sits in crates/testing/tests/binary.rs, in the comment of
  `every_command_refuses_an_argument_it_does_not_declare`. That file is outside the walk, so
  `cargo klarch show` does not list it and `cargo klarch check` will not report it once the goal
  leaves. The one pass re-points it by hand. This is told, not asked.

### `goal@git@a-pure-wrapper-over-git`: Every tool of knowledge-architect that needs to interact with git can do so through one library that wraps git alone

**Kind**: no goal. It is the owner's own example, W2, of a goal "about how the component fits
inside the rest of the project". Removed, nothing the project provides loses its reason to exist:
the checker would wrap git in a module.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head. `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate` already
argues the crate against `rejected@knowledge-architect@git-module-made-public`. It states less
than the goal: not whom the crate serves beyond the checker's library, not that no tool needs a
wrapper of its own, and not that the crate knows nothing of a project's documents. Rows 1, 2 and 5
go to a design issue, Q71a. The session's third
test rewrite, in round 5, did the same and found, in #arg-68, that the
head "stands on its own fact". The first mapping table has no row for it. One decision of its body
is argued by no head: what an operation of the library is. It gets a new head, whose argument the
owner made.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W2. C-git attributes to the owner: "a generic operation of
several steps belongs in the library, not above it; the owner rejected a criterion counting the
tools that need an operation, since it moves design and code after the fact, and approved the
session's criterion".

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "A tool here is any code of knowledge-architect that needs to interact with git: what a project uses, what maintains knowledge-architect itself, and the tests of either, such as a checker ..., or a test that builds a repository as its fixture." | whom the crate serves | design issue: `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate` does not hold the range. It speaks of "Every git invocation of the checker's library" and names five crates; maintenance code, tests and gates are not in it. Q71a |
| 2 | "Through the library, such a tool both reads what a repository holds and has git write, so no tool needs a wrapper of git of its own." | the reason the crate exists | design issue: `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate` does not state it, and `design@knowledge-architect@checker-asks-git-through-the-crate` takes it from this goal, "no tool needs a wrapper of git of its own, per `goal@git@a-pure-wrapper-over-git`". Q71a |
| 3 | "The library wraps git alone: it models operations on a repository, each stated in git's own terms, such as the paths, objects, commits, refs and index it reads or writes, however many git commands one takes, such as rewording a commit's message, or folding a fixup commit into the commit it fixes." | what an operation of the library is | new head: `an-operation-in-git-s-terms-is-the-library-s` |
| 4 | "A step that decides for the matter a tool works on, such as which commits to fold or what a check of the result must find, is the tool's, composed from those operations, however many tools share it." | what is not one | new head: `an-operation-in-git-s-terms-is-the-library-s` |
| 5 | "It knows nothing of a project's documents or of the workflow, and depends on no part of knowledge-architect that does." | the dependency rule | design issue: no head states the rule; a search of crates/git/docs/design.md and of docs/design.md for "knows nothing" finds nothing. Its nearest statement is the argument of `rejected@knowledge-architect@git-module-made-public`, "A tool that needs git would depend on the whole checker to reach it", which `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate` cites. Q71a |
| 6a | measure: "the library's public interface lets each such tool run every git invocation it needs and read what a repository holds" | the statement of row 2 | nowhere: repeats the statement; `issue@git@tools-run-git-past-the-wrapper` holds what is unmet |
| 6b | measure: "holds each operation in git's terms that a tool composes from several invocations, has no function that reads a project's documents or decides for the matter a tool works on" | the criterion of rows 3 and 4 | new head: `an-operation-in-git-s-terms-is-the-library-s` |
| 6c | measure: "and its dependencies hold no part of knowledge-architect that knows a project's documents or the workflow" | the rule of row 5 | nowhere: held by crates/git/Cargo.toml, which declares no dependency. No test holds it: `no_crate_below_the_tools_depends_on_the_checks` of crates/testing/tests/dependency_rules.rs reads `model` and `query` alone |
| 7 | "Requires, for instance: the library offers folding a fixup commit into the commit it fixes as one operation." | an instance of the criterion | new head: `an-operation-in-git-s-terms-is-the-library-s` |
| 8 | "Admits, for instance: a tool that runs git itself, past the library." | a question of how much | existing head: `design@knowledge-architect@checker-asks-git-through-the-crate`, which decides who must ask through the crate and says "A test of the checker may run git itself to build its fixture" |
| 9 | "Rules out, for instance: a function of the library that chooses which commits a tool folds." | an instance of row 4 | new head: `an-operation-in-git-s-terms-is-the-library-s` |

**Head proposed**: `an-operation-in-git-s-terms-is-the-library-s`, in `register@git@design`.

- Title: "An operation stated in git's own terms is a function of the library, however many git
  commands it takes, and a step that decides for a tool's matter is the tool's, however many tools
  share it".
- Goal it cites: none is needed. It applies to the interface the rule of row 5, that the crate
  knows nothing of a project's documents. `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate` holds no such
  rule today, so the head cites it only once Q71a has given that rule a home.
- Nearest rival: an operation enters the library once a second tool needs it.
- The fact that defeats it: the owner's argument, by C-git, that a count of tools "moves design
  and code after the fact": the same operation changes its home when a tool is added, with no
  change to the operation.
- Entry test: test 1, the interface of a published library.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q71. `design@knowledge-architect@checker-asks-git-through-the-crate` leans on this goal for
  `transcript`: "it holds to the same crate since no tool needs a wrapper of git of its own, per
  `goal@git@a-pure-wrapper-over-git`". Re-pointed to `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate`, the
  reason becomes a presumption. Does the owner agree?
- Q71a. Rows 1, 2 and 5 fit no destination: `design@knowledge-architect@git-crate` is the head on
  their subject and states none of the three. My proposal is that the one pass adds the three to
  that head: its argument against `rejected@knowledge-architect@git-module-made-public` covers
  rows 2 and 5. Row 1 is wider than the head, which names the checker's library alone: it counts
  as a tool "what maintains knowledge-architect itself, and the tests of either". Does the owner
  want that range in the head, where
  `design@knowledge-architect@checker-asks-git-through-the-crate` lets a test of the checker run
  git itself?

**Kind**: purpose, once its Component is removed from it. `issue@knowledge-architect@the-mechanical-tasks-goal-omits-what-a-helper-does-better`
records that the owner's lead on the mechanical-tasks goal "gave" this goal. Removed with that goal
unwidened, a command that searches the record by keywords would have no ground. It is not a goal of
its own beside G-A: each says that a tool does what an agent would do at a higher cost.

`design@knowledge-architect@query-crate` argues.

project may query anything the project holds, its git history included, and read what an earlier
query generated", and, on the owner's word, that the goal's sentence "that a tool doing neither
goes in a Component for its own subject leaves".

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "An agent asked to find something in the project can use a tool of this Component, where one is taken in, so that it spends less to reach the answer or reaches a better one." | the outcome | stays in the goal: G-A, whose draft says "at a lower cost to the agent or reach a better one", without "of this Component" |
| 2 | "A tool of this Component queries the project, or reads what an earlier query of this Component generated." | what the crate holds | owner's ruling, into `design@knowledge-architect@query-crate` |
| 3a | measure: "each tool of this Component queries the project or reads what such a query generated" | row 2 | nowhere: repeats the statement |
| 3b | measure: "tests show what it answers over a mock project" | a test | nowhere: held by crates/testing/tests/, which holds the tests of `klarch` over the mock projects, per `design@testing@binary-tests-in-testing`. I did not match a test to each command |
| 3c | measure: "and the installed text's retrospective asks how these tools fared." | how use shows it met | design issue: no head and no text holds it. `design@agent-skills@premortem-as-watch-points` holds the question on "a script or carried by hand over several steps a mechanical task", which watches `goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`; it holds no question on how the query tools fared and names neither goal of `query`. The retrospective skill holds no question in these words: a search of .claude/skills/ and of crates/agent-skills/content/ for "fared" finds nothing. Q72a |
| 4 | "Requires, for instance: a search of the recorded items by keywords, once it is taken in, is a tool of this Component." | a placement | nowhere: held by `issue@query@no-command-searches-the-entries-or-lists-an-audit-s-work`, which the root issue on the mechanical-tasks goal names for that search |
| 5a | "Admits, for instance: a tool that reads git's history; a tool that reads the judgements a grader filled in a work list this Component generated;" | two members | existing head: `design@knowledge-architect@query-crate`, which names `git` "for what `worklist --since` asks of a commit", and `design@query@worklist-writes-and-verifies-the-reports` for the reports |
| 5b | "a tool an agent runs for a result it could reach by hand at a higher cost." | the outcome of row 1 | stays in the goal: G-A |

### `goal@mutate@a-named-mutation-is-checked-safely`: A session checks that its test catches a wrong implementation the session names, over uncommitted work, and the crate's own writes lose nothing of the mutated files

**Kind**: no goal. Its line says it refines
`goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`: it is the generic part of
thaum's tool, taken in. Reworded for the project it holds two statements. That a session can check
that a test discriminates is a step of the development procedure, grounded on
`goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code` (other theme). That the tool loses no
content is grounded on `design@knowledge-architect@no-operation-loses-content`. Neither is a
purpose, a scope or a ranking of the project.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head: the eleven heads of `register@mutate@design` and
`design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host` argue it. The first mapping table's row,
"head", is confirmed. Six heads of `register@mutate@design` cite the goal as what "asks" or "rules
out"; five carry an argument of their own, and `design@mutate@one-file-per-run` rests on the
measure, which the milestone document's table already marks rewritten.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W6. In round 4: "I agree on your list for the
refinements." C-mutate attributes to the owner the readings of the goal: "one mutation per run, a
recorded list not being part of what the crate is for; the two named causes a verdict must tell
apart from a catch; and no limit to one file".

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | "An agent session working in a target project names a mutation of files of the project: the wrong implementation a claim of its work rules out, written for the occasion or kept from an earlier one." | who writes the mutation | existing head: `design@mutate@mutation-command` |
| 2 | "The crate applies it, runs against it a test command the session chooses, and reports whether the command noticed." | what a run does | existing head: `design@mutate@one-command-owns-the-interval` |
| 3 | "The crate never chooses or generates the mutation." | the exclusion | existing head: `design@mutate@mutation-command`, argued against `rejected@mutate@cargo-mutants-instrument` and `rejected@mutate@sweep-shipped`; the owner's reading on one mutation per run goes into it, attributed |
| 4 | "The crate's own writes lose nothing of the mutated files." | the safety rule | existing head: `design@mutate@restore-by-comparison` |
| 5a | "Their content before the mutation, uncommitted edits included, is back in them when the run ends; where the crate cannot put it back ..., or the run was interrupted, that content is kept where the crate's recovery reads it, and the crate writes it back only over the mutant the crate itself wrote," | how it is kept | existing head: `design@mutate@restore-by-comparison` and `design@mutate@record-survives-the-run` |
| 5b | in the same sentence: "or where the file is gone." | the restore's other write | nowhere: held by `path@mutate@CLAUDE.md`, its section "Only one branch of the restore writes to the target": "Its other write is to a target the command deleted, where nothing is there to lose". Neither head's text holds the clause |
| 6 | "What the session's command writes is the session's." | the limit of the rule | existing head: `design@mutate@restore-by-comparison`: "The restore can never remove content the run did not itself put there" |
| 7 | "A verdict is given only about the mutant read back from the files before the command ran." | what a verdict is about | existing head: `design@mutate@one-command-owns-the-interval`: "the read-back line before the command runs" |
| 8 | "The verdict tells the command noticing the mutant apart from a base on which the command already fails, unless the session waived that, and from a mutant that does not build; where it cannot tell them apart, the crate refuses the run or says so in the verdict." | the two causes a verdict tells apart | owner's ruling, into `design@mutate@baseline-runs-by-default` and `design@mutate@build-probe-separates-unbuildable`, which argue one cause each and cite the goal for the other half |
| 9 | measure: "the workflow's setup gives a Rust target project a command that runs the crate" | the delivery | existing head: `design@agent-skills@xtask-pins-checker` |

### `goal@testing@a-consumer-test-uses-what-a-consumer-can`: A test of this Component of an effect a consumer could see uses only what a consumer can, and what it needs and cannot observe there is published

**Kind**: no goal. It is a rule on how the tests of one Component are written. The installed text

`rejected@check@unit-tests-moved-under-tests`. That head grounds it on
`goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code` (other theme).

strength. That head calls the statement "a property of the default shape" and "a guideline per
`goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code`", and decides what the installed text
says to every project. The goal binds the tests of `testing` in this repository outright, and no
head decides that: row 0, Q76b. The first mapping table
has no row for it. No text outside the milestone document cites it.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W2. C-testing attributes to the owner: "a public item only
such a test uses at first is admitted when its effect is one a consumer could see", and "what
becomes of a claim no consumer could see is a design question, outside the goals".

### `goal@xtask@repeated-tasks-are-automated`: The local part of a mechanical task this repository's own instructions require repeatedly is a command of the tool

**Kind**: no goal. W3 again. Its head says the tool "serves this repository, not another target
project", so nothing of it is an outcome of what the project provides. What it decides is where a
task's command sits: in this repository's tool, or in a published crate.

**Fate proposed**: turned into a design head, a new one. The first mapping table has no row for it.
Two heads use its line between a local task and a task of the installed text:
`design@xtask@source-view-in-the-tool` and `design@transcript@transcript-command-reads-any-project`.
One reason at two sites earns a head by test 2 of `primer@design-heads`.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: W3.

**Head proposed**: `a-local-task-is-the-maintenance-tool-s`, in `register@xtask@design`.

- Title: "A task this repository's own instructions require, or whose procedure they fix, is a
  command of this tool, and a task the installed text asks of every project is a published
  crate's".
- Goal it cites: G-A, with the constraint "every session of every target project gets the tool, so
  a task the installed text asks is published, and a task only this repository asks is published
  to nobody".
- Nearest rivals, one each way. A local task as a command of `klarch`, the rival
  `rejected@xtask@source-view-in-the-checker`: `design@xtask@source-view-in-the-tool` gives the
  fact, it "would put in the command line of every adopting project's checker a command that
  either finds no source there or ships this repository's in every binary". A task of the
  installed text as a command of this tool:
  `design@transcript@transcript-command-reads-any-project` gives the fact, the tool "is never
  published, so a project that adopts the workflow, such as thaum, would have no copy".
- Entry test: test 2, one reason at two sites.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q77. `issue@xtask@the-reword-the-fold-and-the-merge-predicate-have-no-command` rests on this
  goal. As a head it is a presumption, and the three commands are then owed by G-A and by
  `issue@knowledge-architect@no-published-command-rewrites-a-branch-s-history` for the two the
  owner took in. Does the owner agree that the merge predicate's command is no longer owed by a
  goal?

stands alone, and the goals of `gates`, `mutate`, `query` (its second), `testing` (its first) and
`transcript` are heads.

### Decided

- An "It refines" line of a goal that stands alone is sent to "nowhere: held by" the goal-setting
  skill, rule 8, since the second mapping table has no row for a line that only leaves. Ground:
  "Decided design", "A goal whose intent is independent of the goal it named stands alone, without
  the line."
- A sentence that belongs with a goal of another theme takes the destination "stays in the goal",
  naming that goal, since the brief allows seven destinations and this is the one whose meaning
  fits. Each is listed under the questions of its goal.
- Rulings of the owner that only a commit message records are used as "owner's ruling"
  destinations, named by the commit's subject, though the brief asks for words from the milestone
  document. Ground: `primer@design-heads`, "A durable record attributes a decision to the owner; it
  does not quote the owner", so the commit message is where these rulings live.
- The three new heads are each placed in a Component's design home, by what each decides. The
  withdrawn head was placed in the root's: which tools one program holds is the root's, per
  `design@agent-skills@dependency-decision-home` as the milestone document reads it.
- The policy on a project's copy of a shipped part is sent to an issue of kind `design` and not to
  a head, since I could name its rival and no fact that defeats it.
- The head `one-program-for-the-tools` is withdrawn and its rule sent to a design issue, for the
  same reason: the fact first given against one program per tool has a counter-instance in the
  tree. Ground: the brief, "If you cannot name a rival and a fact, the destination is
  `design issue`, not a head."
- The owner's rulings on the exception of that rule are sent into
  `design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host`, the head that applies the exception today,
  since an "owner's ruling" destination names the head it bears on and the withdrawn head is none.
- A sentence that fits none of the seven destinations is given "design issue" with a question for
  the owner, and no eighth destination: a statement an existing head on its subject does not hold
  (Q71a, Q76b), a measure's clause no text holds (Q72a), forms and limits no text holds (Q64a,
  Q76a).
- `a-refusal-by-arguments-writes-nothing` is proposed with no goal cited. Ground:
  #arg-68, "some stand on a fact alone".

## Theme: Whom the project serves, and adoption

Questions Q78 to Q96, with Q78a, Q81a, Q81b, Q82a, Q87a, Q93a and Q96a.

Five goals: three of the root's goals home, `register@knowledge-architect@goal`, and the two of `register@create@goal`.
Read at the branch `goals/standard-and-rewrite`, head "The design audit of the first slice of the
goals milestone". Line numbers are those of the two goals homes at that head.

Conventions of the tables:

- A row quotes a sentence verbatim, or gives its first and last words where it is long.
- A sentence with two destinations is split into rows `a`, `b`.
- "nowhere: repeats the statement (row n)" means the sentence repeats a sentence of the same goal's
  body, whose row n carries the destination.
- "stays in the goal (of a goal reference)" names the goal that still states it, where that goal is not
  the one analysed.

Every head named below was opened and read at that head of the branch, unless the row says
"not read".

### `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`: knowledge-architect serves one kind of project: a Rust project whose development AI agents drive, under a linear history

**Kind**: scope. It bounds whom the purposes serve, and it names the designs it excludes: "a mode
for a project in another language", "support for merge commits", "an instruction for a project
whose development persons drive", "a mode of the checker for another harness". Each is a design
that the purpose `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` would otherwise argue
for. Removed, nothing loses its reason to exist and more would be built, as the session said in
round 2 (#arg-40). It means something without any one purpose, since
every goal that says "target project" depends on it, so it is a goal of its own under rule 5.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). The four conditions, the two readings of how much, the named
exclusions, the absence of a guarantee elsewhere, the reading rule for the other goals and the
sentence against narrowing stay. What leaves is the whole apparatus of
the "asks" (rows 12 to 27, 31): an ask is by the goal's own words "added by a design decision of
knowledge-architect", so it is design, and it names parts that exist by a design choice (a gates
library, one command, an invocation). No head argues the asks today, and I can argue none whole:
the head first proposed for them is withdrawn below. The rows go to a design issue, and Q78a asks
the owner whether four of them are scope and stay. The measure and the cases leave. The first
mapping table has no row for this goal: it refines none.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: no word of the milestone document names this goal. Two
bear on its kind and on its second condition:

- round 2 (#arg-31): "Then I add that these model and checker are
  supposed to serve a project keeping design documentation up to date with code, in a project
  developed by LLM agents, and we have the current shape that appears".
- round 3 (#arg-45): "purpose and scope are pretty much opposites: one
  increases what the project is for, the other reduces it. Both are clearly needed to get a
  reasonable scope for one full project."

No word of the owner in the milestone document bears on Rust, on the linear history, on the asks or
on the guarantee. The rewrite must ask the owner on each (questions below).

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled; proposed from the goal's current
text, since no statement of the owner in the milestone document gives its conditions):

> **knowledge-architect serves one kind of project: a Rust project whose development AI agents drive, under a linear history** (draft slug: serves-one-kind-of-project)
>
> knowledge-architect is built for its owner's own projects, open source or private, so that one
> set of agent instructions and mechanical tools serves all of them, and work on one makes each of
> the others more efficient. Ownership is no condition. A target project is a project that holds
> all of these:
>
> - the code it builds and ships is Rust, built by Cargo, apart from the glue its targets need.
>   Files the workflow installs, the configuration of a continuous integration, and scripts kept
>   beside the code are no code it ships;
> - AI agents drive its development: its code, its documents, its entries and its reviews. An edit
>   that a person makes now and then leaves it a target project;
> - its main branch has a linear history, with no merge commit;
> - its owner has decided to use knowledge-architect, from the moment of that decision.
>
> Every other goal that speaks of a project, or of an adopter, means a target project.
>
> Nothing is built or repaired only to serve another project: not a mode for a project in another
> language, not support for merge commits, not instructions for a project whose development
> persons drive. A defect that shows only in such a project is no defect of knowledge-architect,
> and what exists only for such a project may be removed. Anyone may use knowledge-architect in
> another project, with no guarantee that it works there.
>
> The kind is these conditions, not the projects that exist today: that a current target project
> does not need a thing, or does otherwise, is no reason to narrow the conditions, or to limit or
> remove what knowledge-architect covers. That argument still counts for the economy of the
> installed text, for whether an instruction enters it, and for the order of known work.

The draft holds no word on the `claude` harness and none on git: Q80 asks. It holds no word on the
asks, nor on the guarantee where an ask is not followed: Q78a asks.

**Head proposed**: none. The head `asks-bind-every-target-project`, first proposed here for rows 12
to 18, 24 to 27a and 31, is withdrawn. It does not meet what a head stands on:

- The goal it cited gives no constraint for it. "One set of instructions and tools serves every
  target project" is the goal's motive sentence, which the goal says is "the motive; ownership is
  no condition of what follows", and which row 3 records as binding nothing.
- The one fact I can name defeats the rival "no asks" for part of the members only. The installed
  text is one text, the same bytes in every project, per
  `design@knowledge-architect@binary-bundles-workflow`, so an instruction of it that names a
  command, as `cargo x gates`, is false in a project that holds no such command. That argues the
  members about one invocation (rows 20, 21, 24), and `design@agent-skills@maintenance-crate-ask`
  already records it for one ask ("so that a command of the installed text ... names one
  invocation in every project"). It argues nothing for the limit of the guarantee (row 26), for
  what becomes no ask (row 15b), for "still a target project" (row 25), for the ask of row 18, nor
  for the definition of row 31.
- It does not pass test 2 as claimed. The entries that cite the goal for an ask cite it for
  different sentences, not for one reason: the harness, git, how an ask enters and is met, what
  becomes no ask, the gates library.
- The heads it was to list as arguing each ask argue none. `design@check@agents-table` and
  `design@agent-skills@maintenance-crate-ask` take their ask from this goal ("The ask is on the
  owner's word; adding an ask is the owner's, per" this goal),
  `design@agent-skills@gates-convention` recommends, and the git ask has no head.
- No existing head says the same: no head title of any design home concerns the asks.

So the rows are a design issue: reasons respected at several sites that I cannot argue. That
adding an ask goes to the owner "whatever the argument settles" (rows 14b, 22f) is an authority,
held by `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`.

**Re-pointing this fate owes**: these entries cite this goal for a sentence the fate removes from
it. Each is re-pointed, or rewritten, once the owner has ruled where the sentence goes (Q78, Q78a,
Q80), and none if the sentence stays in the goal:

- `design@check@agents-table`: the harness ask and its exclusion (rows 19b, 27b), and the project
  that uses the checker alone (row 18);
- `design@transcript@transcript-reads-claude-code-alone` and
  `design@agent-skills@audit-axis-runs-a-workflow`: the harness ask (row 19b);
- `design@git@repository-variables-dropped` and `design@mutate@record-lives-in-the-git-directory`:
  the git ask (row 19a);
- `design@agent-skills@maintenance-crate-ask`: that adding an ask is the owner's, and how an ask
  is met (rows 14b, 24);
- `design@agent-skills@documentation-presence-is-linted`: how an ask enters (rows 14a, 14b);
- `design@knowledge-architect@mutation-command-host` and `design@agent-skills@diff-mutation-sweep`:
  the quality tool and what becomes no ask (rows 15a, 15b);
- `rejected@agent-skills@rust-project-on-a-local-install`: the gates library (row 20);
- the issue entries that cite the goal for an ask, among them
  `issue@knowledge-architect@the-target-project-and-its-asks-are-stated-to-no-adopter`,
  `issue@gates@the-library-has-no-merge-verdict`,
  `issue@check@an-empty-harness-list-serves-a-state-that-follows-no-ask`,
  `issue@check@configuration-for-several-agent-providers` and
  `issue@agent-skills@heads-argued-from-another-harness`.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q78. The asks leave the goal, as design. An agent may then reverse an ask on an argument and
  report it, unless `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`
  keeps "each addition, tightening or removal of an ask" as the owner's. Do you agree that the asks
  are design, and does that item of the other goal stay?
- Q78a. No head argues the asks, and the one I proposed is withdrawn: I can name a rival and a fact
  only for the members about one invocation. Rows 12 to 18 and 24 to 27a, and row 4b of the goal
  below, are a design issue. Four of them read as scope by the test that keeps rows 32 to 34: the
  asks exist and are to be followed (row 12), a project that does not yet follow one is still a
  target project (row 25), the guarantee holds only as far as the asks are followed (row 26), and
  nothing is built only for a state that follows none (row 27a). Do they stay in the goal, as one
  sentence such as "knowledge-architect asks more of a target project than these conditions; it
  guarantees its working only as far as a project follows what it asks, and builds nothing only to
  serve a state that follows none, other than what helps a project into conformance"? Or are they
  design, argued in a head before the goal loses them?
- Q79. No statement of yours in the milestone document gives the four conditions. Do Rust built by
  Cargo, agents driving the development, a linear history and the owner's decision stay the four,
  in these words?
- Q80. Is "its agents run under the `claude` harness" scope or design? As scope, the goal names it
  with its excluded design, "a mode of the checker for another harness", and only you lift it. As
  design, no head argues it today: `design@check@agents-table` attributes it to this goal, so that
  head gains it with a ground, and an argument may then reopen it. The same question
  holds for "the project is a git repository", which no head states. Either answer changes what
  the goal asks, which the
  slice's spec names as the trigger of `issue@check@configuration-for-several-agent-providers`.
- Q81. The last paragraph of the draft rules out narrowing the conditions, and limiting or removing
  what knowledge-architect covers, on the argument of what a current target project needs. Without
  its last sentence it would also rule out removing an installed instruction on that argument,
  which the goal admits today. So the draft keeps three of the four cases of row 29, which no other
  text holds: the economy of the installed text, whether an instruction enters it, and the order of
  known work. The fourth, the taking in of a generic part, is held by
  `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`. Is that the reach you intend?
- Q81a. Rows 6 and 8 are readings of how much: files the workflow installs, the configuration of a
  continuous integration and scripts kept beside the code are no code a project ships, and an edit
  a person makes now and then leaves it a target project. The draft keeps both inside the
  conditions. Do they stay in the goal, or do you drop them and leave each question of how much to
  an argument?
- Q81b. Row 30: the draft keeps one sentence, "Every other goal that speaks of a project, or of an
  adopter, means a target project." Does it stay in this goal, move to the home's head with the
  definitions the goals share, or leave because every kept goal says "target project" in its own
  words?
- Q82. Row 21: do you want the one merge invocation of every target project argued now, as a head
  of this slice, or kept in the open issue?
- Q82a. Rows 20 and 22a. The goal asks every target project to run its gates from one command
  through the published library, and has a branch merge only once that command passes on its head.
  `design@agent-skills@gates-convention` and the setup skill recommend the command, and neither
  says a merge waits on it. Do you confirm that it is an ask, so that the head states it on your
  word, and is the merge condition argued with row 21?
- Q83. Rows 14b, 15a, 15c, 22f, 29c and 31 lean on goals of other themes
  (`goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`,
  `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`,
  `goal@knowledge-architect@setup-brings-quality-tools`,
  `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`,
  `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`). Each row is lost if the
  analysis of that theme drops the sentence it names. The rows must be read with those analyses.

### `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`: Every target project can adopt knowledge-architect, and a new one is served first

**Kind**: purpose, with a ranking that qualifies it. The purpose: what knowledge-architect ships
serves any target project, with nothing of one project built in. Removed, the manifest in which a
project declares its structure, the declared command and the pin lose their reason to exist: three
heads derive those from it (`design@check@nothing-of-a-project-is-compiled-in`,
`design@check@declared-command`, `design@check@installed-binary-version-check`). The ranking: a new
project comes before an existing one, and a check the workflow needs comes before what conformance
costs an existing project. It holds across decisions.

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded). The mechanisms leave to the heads that already argue them
(nothing compiled in, the declared structure, the grammar, the pin). The first mapping table has no
row: it refines none. Rule 5 is a doubt: the ranking means something without the purpose, so it may
be a goal of its own (Q85 below).

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none in the milestone document. The rewrite of this goal
must ask the owner.

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled; proposed from the goal's current
text):

> **Every target project can adopt knowledge-architect, and a new one is served first** (draft slug: every-target-project-can-adopt-it)
>
> What knowledge-architect provides holds nothing particular to one target project. Each project
> states what is particular to it. A recorded item is cited in one readable form, the same in
> every project, so what an agent learns in one project applies in the next.
> Each project moves to a new version of knowledge-architect when it chooses.
>
> A new target project, which adopts knowledge-architect from its first commit, is served first.
> An existing one can adopt it too, by bringing its documents into conformance. What that costs it
> is never an argument against a check the workflow needs to work well.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q84. No statement of yours in the milestone document bears on this goal. Is the draft's first
  paragraph your intent: nothing of one project built in, and each project moving when it chooses?
- Q85. The ranking of the second paragraph means something without the first. Is it a qualifier of
  this goal, or a ranking goal of its own, between easy adoption and the checks the workflow needs?
- Q86. Row 2: is "this repository is itself a target project" stated in a goal, in
  `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`, or dropped as a fact the tree shows?
- Q87. Row 5: the grammar sentence touches the goals on references of another theme. Is "what an
  agent learns in one project applies in the next" kept here, or there?
- Q87a. Rows 5a and 5b: the clause of Q87 rests on the sameness of citations across projects, so
  the draft keeps "cited in one readable form, the same in every project" with it. That names a
  kind of interface, which rule 2 keeps in a goal only as a purpose. Is it your intent that a
  recorded item is cited the same way in every project, and that the form is readable, which no
  head argues? Or does the clause stand on another premise, which you give?

### `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`: Adopting knowledge-architect is easy, with documentation that is clear and reaches its reader

**Kind**: purpose. Removed, what knowledge-architect provides to an adopter alone loses its reason
to exist: the crates.io page of each crate (`design@knowledge-architect@crates-io-page-file` says
"This serves" it), the rule that a finding names its repair
(`design@check@finding-names-the-repair`: "the finding is what a consumer reads first"), and the
command that creates a project (`design@create@init-command`: "its creation is where a person or
an agent starts").

**Fate proposed**: kept (reworded), and it receives the outcome of
`goal@create@a-project-adopts-by-one-command`. The list of where the documentation sits, the
measure and the cases leave. The sentence "An adopter who had to read the source or ask
knowledge-architect's owner" is written as a measure, but it is the sharpest statement of the
outcome, and I keep it as one. The first mapping table has no row: it refines none.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none in the milestone document. The rewrite must ask the
owner.

No head says it: the body of `design@agent-skills@adopting-existing-docs` names no check. The same sentence is row 13 of the goal above |

| 9 | "Requires, for instance: the README of each published package states, with an example, what an adopter of it does." | a case of rows 2 and 3 | nowhere: repeats the statement (rows 2 and 3) |
| 10 | "Admits, for instance: an adopter who reads the source by choice." | a case of row 8 | nowhere: repeats the statement (row 8) |
| 11 | "Rules out, for instance: a step of adoption stated only in the source of a crate." | a case of row 8 | nowhere: repeats the statement (row 8) |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard** (draft, not ruled; proposed from the current text of
this goal and of `goal@create@a-project-adopts-by-one-command`):

> **Adopting knowledge-architect is easy, with documentation that is clear and reaches its reader** (draft slug: adoption-is-easy)
>
> A person or an agent adopting knowledge-architect in a target project, new or existing, finds
> what to do in its documentation, where they already look for it, in plain words and with
> examples, without reading its source or asking its owner. One command leaves the project with
> every file knowledge-architect requires of it, ready to be completed. Adopting loses nothing the
> project wrote.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q88. No statement of yours in the milestone document bears on this goal. Is the draft your intent?
- Q89. "One command" is a kind of interface. Rule 2 keeps it in a goal only as a purpose, and asks
  the drafter to ask you. Is "by one command" your intent, or is the intent "with little work",
  the one command being `design@create@init-command`'s way?
- Q90. "Adopting loses nothing the project wrote" comes from the goal of `create`. See its
  questions below.
- Q91. Is this goal one intent with `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`?
  I keep them apart: one says who can adopt and what ranks first, the other how hard adopting is.

### `goal@create@a-project-adopts-by-one-command`: A project, new or existing, adopts knowledge-architect by one command, which leaves every file the checker requires ready to be completed and loses nothing the project wrote

**Kind**: no goal of its own. A Component states no goal. Reworded as an outcome of the project
as a whole, "adopting takes one command and loses nothing the project wrote", it qualifies the
purpose `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`: it says how easy, and what adopting never
costs. Removed, `klarch init` does not lose its reason to exist, since `design@create@init-command`
already argues it from that purpose and from `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`.
What would lose its ground is narrower: the refusal of a run that would remove a file the project
wrote, which no head argues and which `issue@create@init-outside-a-worktree-deletes-an-owned-file-silently`
records as unbuilt.

**Fate proposed**: merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy` for two sentences of
its draft, which hold three statements (one command; every required file stands, ready to be
completed; nothing the project wrote is lost), the rest to heads of `create` that exist. I overturn
the row of the first mapping table, which says "head", in part. The row is right that the goal is
grounded on another: five heads of `register@create@design` and one of `register@check@design`
already hold its shape. It misses
that one outcome in it is held against two heads today. The issue says, on the owner's word, that
`design@create@init-asks-no-confirmation` and `design@create@init-keeps-existing-files` conflict
with the goal. Turned into a head, the goal no longer prevails over them, as the slice's spec
notes. Kept as a sentence of a goal, it does.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none verbatim in the milestone document. The session, round
1 (#arg-25), counted it among the goals that "read as product
outcomes". The issue of `create` attributes a ruling to the owner without quoting it: "On the
owner's word that is a breach of" this goal. The rewrite must ask the owner.

| # | sentence | what it states | destination |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 1 | "A person or an agent adopting knowledge-architect runs one command." | one command | stays in the goal (of `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`), if the owner rules it an outcome; otherwise existing head: `design@create@init-command` |
| 2a | "Every file the checker requires then stands, ready to be completed," | the state the command leaves | stays in the goal (of `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`), whose draft says "every file knowledge-architect requires of it, ready to be completed". Which files those are is `design@create@init-command` ("writes the manifest ... then every document the root Component, each Component it lists and the plans directory owe") |
| 2b | "and the checker can read the project whole." | the state the command leaves, for the checker | existing head: `design@create@init-command` ("writes the indexes once phases 1 to 3 find nothing, and runs the check, whose verdict is the run's") |
| 3 | "A full pass of the checker is not asked of this command: what remains is work the checker names." | how much the command reaches | existing head: `design@create@init-keeps-existing-files` ("the check then reports what it lacks") |

The head argues the opposite today: a run that "overwrites no hand-written file outside the installer's namespace loses nothing an install would not", and installs "the shipped set as `design@check@owned-namespace-check` lets every install do". The issue of `create` says the head "conflicts with the goal until this work rewrites it", and records the refusal as unbuilt. The sentence of row 4, kept in a goal, still prevails over the head (Q93a) |

**Draft of the goal under the new standard**: the last two sentences of the draft of
`goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy` above. Draft, not ruled.

**Re-pointing this fate owes**: `design@knowledge-architect@create-crate` ("serves
`goal@create@a-project-adopts-by-one-command` and ..."), `rejected@create@init-refused-outside-a-repository-root`
and the issue of `create` cite this goal. Each is re-pointed to
`goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`. The first and the issue lean on the goal's words and
are rewritten, not only re-pointed.

**Questions for the owner**

- Q92. Is "adopting loses nothing the project wrote" an outcome you hold as a goal, in
  `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`? If it is a head only, an argument may reverse it,
  and the two heads the issue names no longer lose to it.
- Q93. Rows 6, 11 and 17 are recorded as your rulings: the bytes rank over a readable project, and

- Q93a. Row 5b, and row 4b of the goal below: a run that would remove a file the project wrote

  It is recorded as your ruling, into that head, which the work of the issue rewrites. Do you

- Q94. Do you want the statement general: "no command of knowledge-architect that writes into a

  of the installer's namespace that the version does not ship". The draft says "adopting" only.

### `goal@create@a-project-gains-a-component-by-one-command`: A project gains a Component by one command, which leaves the files the checker requires of it ready to be completed and loses nothing the project wrote

**Kind**: no goal. It names a Component, a part that exists by a design choice, and a command.
Reworded as an outcome of the project as a whole, "a target project adds a part by one command",
it is a means to `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-components` and to
`goal@knowledge-architect@mechanical-tasks-are-the-tool-s`, both of another theme. Removed, nothing
loses its reason to exist: `design@create@component-command` and
`design@create@component-manifest-edit` hold the shape, the second with its rival ("Printing the
line for the user to add was the rival: the command could then not finish in one run").

**Fate proposed**: removed. No new head. Every sentence goes to a head that exists, with these
exceptions: rows 3, 4b and 8 are rulings of the owner that a head gains, since
`design@create@component-command` holds no statement that `new-component` loses nothing the project
wrote, and `design@create@init-keeps-existing-files` says today "the install writes and deletes
there"; and row 6b is a clause of the measure that no test holds. The first mapping
table has no row: it holds no line "It refines". One weakness I name: `design@create@component-command`
cites no goal and argues the command against no rival. If the owner wants its existence argued,
the head is brought to the rules on design heads when it is touched; slice 2 rewrites it already,
per the milestone document's table.

**Words of the owner that bear on it**: none in the milestone document. The issue of `create` says
of the breach it records that this goal "is met no better".

| `goal@create@a-project-gains-a-component-by-one-command` | no goal | removed; every sentence to an existing head, three of them as rulings a head gains (rows 3, 4b, 8), but one clause of the measure (row 6b) |

Counts: 5 goals, 5 sections. 0 new head proposed; 1 withdrawn. 18 rows sent to a design issue that
no head answers: 16 of the first goal (rows 12, 13, 14a, 15b, 16, 17, 18, 19a, 19b, 21, 22a, 24,
25, 26, 27a and 27b; rows 19a, 19b and 27b only if Q80 rules them design), row 4b of the second
goal and row 6b of the fifth. 1 row sent to an issue that exists (row 35 of the first goal).

### Texts above the first goal

**`register@knowledge-architect@goal`, lines 3 to 28.** This theme owns two parts of it.

- "knowledge-architect is the common toolchain of one kind of project, the target project that
  `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` defines." It states what the project is
  for as a whole, which the new standard keeps in the home's head and the owner rules like a goal.
  It stays, in the README of `path@elsewhere@docs/goals/`. The rest of that paragraph
  belongs to other themes.
- "These words hold in these senses for the goals of this home and of every Component". The words
  "and of every Component" leave: no Component states a goal. "Target project" is defined by the
  scope goal, since the definition is that goal's outcome. "Quality tool" (row 15a) goes to the
  head only if two kept goals use it.

**`register@create@goal`, lines 3 to 5.**

- "Every goal here serves one purpose: a project gets by one command the files the checker
  requires of it, ready to be completed, and loses nothing it wrote." It leaves with the home's
  entries. Its content is the two sentences merged into `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`.
  Destination: stays in the goal (of that goal).
- "What the checker requires of a project is what the project declares to it, the documents and
  the homes of each Component, and the installed text." Destination: existing head:
  `design@create@init-command`, which derives the list from `Manifest::required_documents` and
  `Anchors::of`.
- "A file at a path this version's installed text is installed to does not count as one the
  project wrote, even where it was edited by hand." Destination: existing head:
  `design@check@owned-namespace-check`.
- "A file the install would remove counts as one the project wrote, one an older version installed
  included." Destination: owner's ruling, into `design@create@init-keeps-existing-files`. It
  conflicts today with `design@check@owned-namespace-check` ("The install deletes every file of the
  installer's namespace that the version does not ship"), as the issue of `create` records. It is
  the reading on which rows 11 and 17 rest.

### Decided

- The asks of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` are sorted as design, on the
  goal's own words ("added by a design decision of knowledge-architect") and rule 2 of the
  standard. The owner rules it (Q78 of that goal).
- No head is proposed for the asks. The head `asks-bind-every-target-project` is withdrawn: the
  goal gives it no constraint, its one fact argues the members about one invocation only, and the
  heads it was to list take their ask from the goal. The rows are a design issue, and Q78a asks
  whether four of them are scope.
- The two readings of how much (rows 6 and 8 of the first goal), its reading rule for the other
  goals (row 30) and three of the four cases where the argument still counts (rows 29a, 29b, 29d)
  are kept in the draft until the owner rules, since no other text holds them (Q81, Q81a, Q81b).
- The draft of `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` keeps "cited in one
  readable form, the same in every project", since the clause it keeps rests on it (Q87a).
- The sentence "An adopter who had to read the source or ask knowledge-architect's owner", written
  as a measure, is kept as an outcome of `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`.
- The row "head" of the first mapping table for `goal@create@a-project-adopts-by-one-command` is
  overturned in part, on the issue of `create` that records the goal prevailing over two heads.
- `goal@create@a-project-gains-a-component-by-one-command` is sorted as no goal, with no new head,
  since `design@create@component-command` and `design@create@component-manifest-edit` hold every
  sentence but the owner's ruling on loss, and one clause of its measure that no test holds (row 6b).
- A row whose sentence repeats a body sentence of the same goal is written "nowhere: repeats the
  statement (row n)", which reads the seventh destination as covering the goal's body, not its
  title alone.
- Where a sentence is held by a goal of another theme, the destination is written "nowhere: held
  by" that goal, with the risk named, since the brief gives no destination for it.

### Met outside the task

- `design@check@agents-table` says the goal "admits" a target project that uses the checker alone;
  the goal says such a project "does not follow this ask". The head's word is loose. Dropped here:
  the head is rewritten when the asks move.
- `design@check@the-regime-has-no-opt-out` cites `goal@check@document-rules-are-checked`, of
  another theme, for a constraint close to row 9 of the second goal. Left to that theme.
- `design@create@component-command` cites no goal and names no rival. Named in its goal's section;
  slice 2 rewrites the head.
- `issue@knowledge-architect@the-target-project-and-its-asks-are-stated-to-no-adopter` quotes the
  goal's measure and its list of asks; it is rewritten when the goal is, as the slice's spec says
  of issues that quote a measure.
- `init --vcs none` writes a project that is no git repository, which that issue already records.
  Known; nothing opened.
- The harness refused the Write tool for this file ("Subagents should return findings as text"),
  so it was written through the shell, as the task asks for a file at this path.
